However, one will not with reason give the name of "just appearance" to any other of those who have reigned if not to Christ; He is the sun of justice; He made the rays of the science of God shine for us; He also practiced equity and justice on the earth, when He extended over all the earth His laws (νόμος) of an absolutely superior justice; simultaneously under His days Judah was saved and Israel inhabited in security. Indeed, when from the time of Solomon the people had split into two parts, Israel and Judah, there were until the end two separate kingdoms; but in a general way all those who were separated submitted and obeyed equally to the preaching of Christ and to His kingdom and they received a single yoke. However, because of Judah which is interpreted "he who confesses" and of Israel which is interpreted "the intelligence that sees God", one will call "Judah who is saved" those who now have approached the Gospel and who hold


[p. 293] {#po.8.p293}
ܕܐܬܦܪܩ܂. ܠܗܶܢܘܢ ܕܗܟܐ ܐܬܩܪܒܘ ܠܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ.. ܘܐܚܝܕܝܢ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܿܘܕܝܢ. ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܪܐ ܬܟܝܠܐܝܬ܂. ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܟܕܘ ܗܝܡܢܘ ܘܥܒܪܘ ܒܬܐܘܪܝܐ. ܘܒܗܕܐ ܡܣܬܟܠܝܢ ܐܘ ܟܝܬ ܚܙܝܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ܇ ܘܡܫܪܪܝܢ ܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܘܬܟܝܠܝܢ܇. ܘܐܝܟ ܗܽܘ ܕܥܡܪܝܢ ܘܡܬܬܢܝܚܝܢ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܚܕܥܘ. ܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܓܡ̈ܐ.. ܚܕ ܦܓܪܐ ܕܥܕܬܐ ܡܩܝܡܝܢ.. ܘܬܚܝܬ ܚܕ ܪܥܝܐ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܗܘ ܕܐܦ ܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܪܡܝܐ ܕܡܬܩܪܐ ܝܘܨܕܩ.. ܗܿܘ ܟܝܬ ܕܡܬܝܕܥ ܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܟܕ ° ܠܠܫܢܐ ܝܘܢܝܐ ܡܬܦܫܩ. ܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܐܒܐ: ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܚܟܡܬܐ ܘܚܝܠܐ. ܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܫܡܥܝܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܠܦܘܠܘܣ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ ܟܬܒ ܠܩܘܪܢܬܝܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܠܢ ܚܟܡܬܐ ܡܢ ° ܐܠܗܐ ܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܟܝܬ ܘܩܕܝܫܘܬܐ ܘܦܘܪܩܢܐ. ܟܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܪܡܝܐ ܢܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܢܒܝܐܝܬ ܡܛܠ ܝܫܘܥ ܘܐܸܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܬܩܪܐ ܝܘܨܕܩ: ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ: ܘܝܬܝܪ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܘܢܗܝܪܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܗܘ ܕܒܝܢܬܗܝܢ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܠܐ: ܠܗ ܠܗܽܘ ܕܡܬܢܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܐܡܪ ܘܐܦ ܒܗܝܢ ܒܥܝܢ̈ܐ ܐܫܬܘܕܥ: ܡܕܢܚܗ ܗܽܘ ܕܗܘܝܢ ܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܐ: ܘܗܫܐ ܡܢܢ ܡܬܚܙܐ݀ ܐܬܢܗܪ.. ܡܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܐܙܥܩܢ. ܗܼܘ ܒܢܒܝ̈ܐ. ܟܕ ܐܝܟ ܗܽܘ ܕܒܨܒܥܐ ܡܚܘܐ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܡܿܪ.. ܗܼܘ ܗܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ
the place of those who confess, and one will call "Israel who inhabits in security" those who already have believed and have passed through contemplation (θεωρία), who by that know or see God, who are confirmed by science and who are in security and so to speak who inhabit and rest in what they have known. But these two camps (τάγμα) constitute the unique body of the Church and are placed under the sole authority of Christ, whom Jeremiah also declares with reason to be called Josedec, which, translated into the Greek language, signifies "the justice of God". And Christ is the justice of God the Father as well as the wisdom and the power. Listen indeed to Paul who writes to the Corinthians: Who has become for us wisdom by the fact of God, and justice and sanctification and redemption. When Jeremiah emitted this prophecy about Jesus and said: He will be called Josedec, that is to say "the justice of God", he recognized very clearly and very plainly Him whom he was prophesying and in the midst of his words and also with his own eyes so to speak, and he was enlightened by His appearance which then was to come and which now we see. Also in his joy he cried out: It is in the prophets, by showing Him thus as with the finger and by saying: It is He who has been in the prophets and who


[p. 294] {#po.8.p294}
ܗܽܘ ܕܒܢܒܝ̈ܐ ܗܼܘܐ܆. ܘܕܢܬܢܒܝܘܢ ܟܠܝܢ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ ܢܦܚ ܒܗܘܢ ܘܐܥܒܕ ܡܫܚܠܦܐܝܬܐ܀
inspired them and who made them in a diverse way emit these prophecies which relate to His own person.
ܡܛܠ ܗܢܐ ܕܢܝܼܚܐ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܒܪܢܫ ܡܛܠܬܢ.. ܐܦ ܙܟܪܝܐ ܢܒܝܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܐܠܗܐܝܬ ܐܡܿܪ.. ܗܐ ܓܒܪܐ܂ ܕܢܚܐ ܫܡܐ ܠܗ. ܘܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ ܡܢܗ ܢܕܢܚ. ܐܘܝ ܟܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܚܬܝܬܘܬܗ̇ ܘ ܕܢܒܝܘܬܐ. ܡܝܼܢܘ ܕܠܐ ܢܫܒܚ ܠܗܘ ܕܩܕܡ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܢܝܚܐ ܒܝܕ ܥܒܕ̈ܐ ܒܝ̈ܬܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܓܪܝܕܐܝܬ ܡܠܠ ܐܢܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ܇. ܘܒܝܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܝܐ ܒܥ̈ܒܕܐ ܡܿܠܝ ܐܢܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܩܼܕܡ ܐܬܐܡܪ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܝܼܚܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܢܒܝܐ ܫܡܐ ܠܗ. ܢܘܗܪܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܐܒܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܡܢܗܪ ܠܟܠ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܕܐܬܐ ܠܥܠܡܐ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ܂ ܗ̇ܘ ܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܦ ܐܬܩܪܝ. ܘܕܝܥܝܬܗ ܡܿܢ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܕܫܝܼܡܫܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܗܳܝ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܢܢܗܪ ܘܙܠܝܩ̈ܝܘܗܝ ܢܪܡܐ ܒܙܟܠܝܢ ܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܠܡܬܢܗܪܘ.. ܘܠܡܒܫܬܦܘ ܒܢܨܝܚܘܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܬܡܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܝܼܡܫܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ: ܢܘܗܪܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܠܐ ܡܬܩܪܒܢܐ ܓܒܪܐ ܗܼܘܐ: ܟܕ ܩܝܼܒܠ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܢܬܒܣܪ ܘܕܢܬܒܪܢܫ ܡܛܠܬܢ. ܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ ܕܢܼܚ. ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܢܝܼܣܒ ܕܡܝܼܘܬܐ ܕܥܒܕܐ: ܘܡܿܟܝܟ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܨܒܝܢܐܝܬ.. ܡܢ ܡܬܚܬܚܘܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܗܟܝܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܡܟܟܬܐ ܕܨܢܥ. ܘܒܐܝܕܐ ܒܝܕܐ ܚܘܝ ܗܼܘ ܠܗܢ. ܘܠܚܝܼܪܬܐ ܐܢܗܪ
Concerning this appearance of the Word who became man for us, the prophet Zechariah also says in a quite divine way: Behold a man whose name is Orient and he will rise from beneath him. Ah! how great is the exactitude of the prophecy! Who would not praise Him who so many years before pronounced clearly these words by His own servants and who in these last times accomplished in fact the words said in advance? His name is Orient, says indeed the prophet. He is the light of the light of the Father, that which enlightens every man coming into this world; that is why He also bore the name of what He is. The property of light and of the sun is to enlighten from above and to project their rays on what must be enlightened and have a part in the clarity that comes from it. But because the sun of justice, the inaccessible light, became man, by taking upon Himself to be incarnate and to become man for us, He rose from below; for when He took a form of a servant and humbled Himself voluntarily, He rose from a lowering which is thus very humble, He showed Himself little by little


[p. 295] {#po.8.p295}
ܐܝܟ ܐܢܫ ܢܐܡܪ ܒܟܠܗ̇ ܐܠܗܘܬܐ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܙܟܪܝܐ݀ ܟܕ ܩܿܕܡ ܗܘܐ ܡܿܟܝܪܙ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܗܕܐ ܬܕܡܘܪܬܐ ܐܡܼܪ.. ܗܐ ܓܒܪܐ݀ ܕܢܼܚܐ ܫܡܐ ܠܗ. ܘܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ ܡܢܗ
and finally He shone so to speak with all the divinity. That is why Zechariah, when he announced in advance this prodigy, said: Behold a man whose name is Orient and he will rise from beneath him.
ܢܕܢܚ܀ ܠܗܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܼܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ.. ܐܦ ܕܘܝܕ݀ ܟܕ ܠܗ ܠܗܕܐ ܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܢܝܐܝܬ ܦܝܼܫܩ.. ܕܡܢ ܡܥܪܒܐ ܕܢܝܼܚ ܐܡܿܝܪ. ܘܥܡ ܝܘܚܢܢ ܡܥܡܕܢܐ ܠܘܬ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܢܫܬܘܘܢ ܠܕܢܼܚܗ ܡܿܙܥܩ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ. ܛܝܒܘ ܐܘܪܚܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ܂. ܬܪ̈ܝܨܐ ܥܒܕܘ ܠܫܒܝ̈ܠܐ ܕܝܠܗ. ܥܒܕܘ ܓܝܪ ܐܘܪܚܐ ܐܡܪ ܠܗܘ ܕܪܟܒ ܥܠ ܡܥܪܒܐ.. ܡܪܝܐ ܫܡܐ ܠܗ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܐܦܢ ܥܠ ܡܣܟܢܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܪܟܝܼܒ: ܘܠܫܘܒܚܐ ܒܚܝܬܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܡܕܒܪܢܐܝܬ ܚܦܝ: ܐܝܟ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢ ܡܥܪܒܐ ܕܢܚ܂. ܐܠܐ ܒܪܡ܂ ܡܪܝܐ ܫܡܐ ܠܗ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ݀ ܘܟܕ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܗܼܘܐ܂. ܠܐ ܫܼܕܗܿ ܠܗ̇ܝ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܘܐܠܗܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦܢ ܢܬܠ ܕܕܢܚ ܫܡܫܐ ܡܢ ܡܥܪܒܐ.. ܗܼܘ ܡܿܢ܂ ܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܒܗܘܪܐ. ܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܂. ܕܠܡܥܪܒܐ ܡܘܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܕܢܚܐ. ܒܗ ܟܕ ܒܗ ܒܙܢܐ݀ ܐܦ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ: ܟܕ ܡܥܪܒܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܘܒܪܐ ܕܝܠܢ: ܟܕ ܐܘܟܡ ܒܚܛܝܼܬܐ: ܘܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܓܠܝܙ ܡܢ ܣܥܘܪܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ܂. ܡܕܢܚܐ ܥܒܕܗ. ܟܕ ܒܝܼܪܡ ܠܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܕܠܗ̇ܝ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܡܬܒܣܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ:. ܗܿܝ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܥܒܕ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ
Speaking of this rising which takes place from below, David explained this thought in a different way and he said that He rose in the west. And he also cries out with John the Baptist to those who were to be the witnesses of His rising: Prepare the way of the Lord, make straight his paths, by saying: Prepare the way for Him who has mounted upon the west; His name is the Lord. Indeed, although He has mounted upon our poverty and has veiled His particular glory according to the economy, He rose in some way in the west; but nevertheless His name is the Lord; for even when He became man, He did not renounce being Lord and God. Indeed, although (the Psalmist) declares that the sun rises in the west, this one does not become itself obscure; but on the contrary he shows that the west is the east. In the same way, as our state was the west, since it was black by the fact of sin and was deprived of divine action, the Word of God made it the east, when He blessed our passage to this new condition where, by His incarnation


[p. 296] {#po.8.p296}
ܘܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܛܒܐ ܒܝܬ ܒܢ̈ܝܢܫܐ ܐܬܕܒܪ:. ܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܘܢܟܦܘܬܐ܆. ܘܚܘܕܪܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܦܪܟܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ܀
by which were operated the works of light and all that is good among men, we were to become justice, purity, and the sum of the other virtues.
ܘܐܟܙܢܐ ܕܡܿܠܟܐ ܕܨܒܐ ܕܢܚܙܘܩ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܙܥܘܪܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܝܥܬܐ: ܘܕܐܦܠܐ ܣܟ ܣܦܩܐ ܠܡܣܝܒܪܘ ܠܡܐܬܝܬܗ: ܐܦܢ ܙܒܝ̈ܢܝܢ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܢ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܢܩܦ ܘܡܦܣܩ ܠܣܘܓܐܗ̇ ܕܢܦܝܚܘܬܐ ܕܦܐܢܛܐܣܝܐ' ܘܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܒܚܕܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܣܬܝܒܪܢܐ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܐ: ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ݀ ܕܠܘ ܡܿܠܟܐܝܬ ܢܥܘܠ: ܘܐܦܠܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܢܟܦܘܪ ܒܝܗܝ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܗ܂. ܗܟܝܢܐ ܐܦ ܒܪܐ ܘܡܝܠܬܗ ܕܐܒܐ:. ܗܽܘ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪܟܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟܢܐ܆. ܟܕ ܨܒܐ ܕܐܢܫܐܝܬ ܢܐܬܐ ܠܗܢܐ ܥܠܡܐ.:. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܠܘܬܗ ܢܘܛܝܼܦܬܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܣܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܢܒܝ̈ܐ ̈ ܟܕ ° ܐܠܗܐܝܬ ܡܿܠܝܐ ܠܗ ܠܗܢܐ܇ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܡܫܟܚܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܢܬܡܿܟܟ ܡܢ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܒܝܬܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܘܝܬܝܪ ܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܢܐܬܐ̇. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܗܿܝ ܕܢܣܐܬܪܩ ܘܒܝܕ ܗܕܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܡܬܩܪܒܢܐ.. ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ܇. ܒܪܡ ܠܐ ܡܬܦܚܡܢܐܝܬ ܘܒܙܢܐ ܡܕܡ ܝܕܝܥܐ ܘܦܪܝܫܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܢܘܢ ܐ̱ܚܪ̈ܢܐ܇. ܒܝܕ ܬܪܥܐ ܡܕܡ« ܐܠܗܝܐ ܘܕܝܬܝܪ ܡܿܠܟܝ ܗܢܘ
Just as a king who wants to go to a city small, unknown, and absolutely incapable of supporting his coming, often makes himself small and suppresses the greatness of the pride of the imagination (φαντασία) or of the glory that surrounds him, in order to be supportable to this city, and yet he cannot not enter as a king and renounce in a general way what he is; likewise also the Son and the Word of the Father, the incomprehensible and the infinite, wanted to come under a human form into this world which is beside Him like a drop (suspended) from a bucket according to the expression of one of the prophets, since He fills it in a divine way, and as much as it was possible for Him He humbled Himself from His own glory and came to humiliation, - it is in that indeed that He emptied Himself and that He thus became accessible, - (but) after all that however, in an incomparable way and in a remarkable and special manner that surpasses all the others, He entered into our world by a divine and very royal door, that is to say by virginity,


[p. 297] {#po.8.p297}
ܕܝܢ ܒܬܘܠܘܬܐ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܥܼܠ.. ܟܕ ° ܡܢ ܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܘܡܢ ܚܠܕܬܐ ܐܠܗܐ
by being born in the flesh of the Holy Spirit and of the Virgin Mother of God.
ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܒܒܣܪ ܐܬܝܠܕ܀ ܠܗܢܐ ܚܠܕܐ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܙܪܥܐ܂. ܕܪܢܚ ܡܢ ܫܒܝܛܐ ܕܝܗܘܕܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܡܢܗ ܢܒܚܬܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܪܝܡ ܒܚܘܒܠܐ: ܐܦ ܝܥܩܘܒ ܪܝܫ ܐܒ̈ܝܗܬܐ݀ ܩܕܡ ܗܘܐ ܐܡܿܪ.. ܟܕ ܒܡ̈ܠܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܟܠܗ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܪܐܙܐ ܚܕܼܒܫ. ܐܡܼܪ ܓܝܪܢ. ܓܘܪܝܐ ܕܐܪܝܐ ܝܗܘܕܐ܂. ܡܢ ܡܘܥܝܬܐ ܒܪܝ ܣܠܩܬ. ܟܕ ܓܘܪܝܐ ܡܿܢ ܕܐܼܪܝܐ ܟܢܝܚܗ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ.. ܡܛܠ ܡܠܟܝܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܩܪܒܢܘܬܐ. ܝܗܘܕܐ ܕܝܢ.. ܡܛܠ ܕܡܢ ܫܒܝܛܐ ܕܝܗܘܕܐ ܢܓܕ ܗܘܐ ܫܼܘܝܐ ܓܝܢܣܐ ܒܒܣܪ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗܝ ܕܚܠܦܝܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܚܕܐ ܦܐܪܝܣܝܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܘܼܕܝ ܘܐܡܼܪ. ܡܘܕܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܐܒܐ ܡܪܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ ܘܕܐܪܥܐ̇. ܕܛܫܝܼܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܚܟܝ̈ܡܐ ܘܣܟܘ̈ܠܬܢܐ܆̈ ܘܓܝܼܠܝܬ ܐܢܝܢ ܠܢܒܪ̈ܐ܇ ܐܝܢ ܐܒܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܝܗܘܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܂. ܐܡܼܝܪܢܢ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܟܕܘ.. ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܝܘܕܐ ܡܬܦܫܩ. ܗܽܘ ܕܠܘܬܗ: ° ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܗܘ ܕܚܠܦܝܢ ܐܘܼܕܝ: ܟܕ ܒܥܝ̈ܢܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܝܩܬܐ ܘܕܣܕܝܩܢ ܚܙܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ! ܕܥܬܝ̈ܕܢ݀ ܚܐܪ ܗܘܐ ܝܥܩܘܒܪ ܐܸܡܼܪ.. ܡܢ ܡܘܥܝܬܐ ܒܪܝ ܣܝܼܠܩܬ. ܟܕ ܡܘܥܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ.. ܠܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܩܪܐ܆. ܗܝ
This extraordinary and seedless birth, by which He rises from the tribe of Judah from which Mary descended by her family, Jacob the first of the patriarchs predicted, by enclosing in a few words all the richness of this mystery. He said indeed: Judah is a young lion; it is from my race, my son, that you have mounted. He calls Christ "a young lion" because He possesses royal dignity and inaccessibility, and "Judah" because He drew from the tribe of Judah the same race (γένος) in the flesh and because He confessed for us who have no freedom (παῤῥησία) before God, by saying: I confess to you, Father, Lord of heaven and of earth, that you have hidden these things from the wise and the intelligent, and that you have revealed them to children. Yes, Father, (I confess to you) that you have willed it so. Now Judah, we have already said, is interpreted "he who confesses". While he was considering Him, I mean Him who confessed for us, with eyes that despite the distance saw the future in advance, he said: It is from my race, my son, that you have mounted, by calling his own race the Holy Virgin from whom, thus


[p. 298] {#po.8.p298}
ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܫܒܫܬܐ̈. ܡܫܝܚܐ ܣܝܓܘܠܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܠܐ ܙܪܥܐ ܣܝܼܠܩ ܘܐܘܥܝܚ܀ ܐܝܟܐ ܘܟܝܠ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܡܥܝܢ.. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܒ̈ܥܠܝ ܫܡܐ ܕܫܢܝܘܬܗ ܕܐܘܛܘܟܐ. ܐܪ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܠܪܝܫ ܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܢܬܬܦܝܣܘܢ ܕܐܡܿܪ: ܕܡܢ ܡܘܥܝܬܐ ܒܪܝ ܣܠܩܬ: ܟܕ ܓܚܟܝܢ ܥܠ ܫܛܘܪܘܬܐ ܕܦܐܢܛܐܣܝܐ!: ܕܡܢ ܐܘܣܝܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܢܘܕܘܢ: ܦܓܪܗ ܩܕܝܫ ܒܟܠ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ.. ܒܪܬ ܓܢܣܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܝܠܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܪܝܡ: ܐܦ ܝܥܩܘܒ ܐܒܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܝܠܗܿ.. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܡܢܗ ܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܕܛܝܢܐ ܡܬܩܢܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܸܡܿܪ ܟܬܒܐ ܕܐܝܘܒ. ܘܗܽܘ ܕܬܿܟܢܢ ܘܓܝܼܒܠܢ. ܠܘ ܠܐܚܪܬܐ܂. ܐܠܐ܂ ܠܗܿ ܠܓܒܝܠܬܐ ܗܺܝ ܕܢܦܠܬ ܘܩܝܲܒܠܬ ܠܚܝܒܠܐ ܕܚܛܝܼܝܬܐ ܐܬܐ ܕܢܬܪܨ ܘܢܓܒܘܠ ܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ. ܒܝܕ ܡܬܒܣܪܢܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ:. ܟܕ ܐܝܟ ܚܡܝܪܐ ܐܪܡܝ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܒܓܒܝܠܬܐ ܟܠܗܿ ܕܓܢܣܐ ܐܢܫܝܐ:. ܘܗܼܘܐ ܐܕܡ ܬܪܝܢܝܐ܇. ܘܦܪܩ ܠܢ ܒܩܝܼܡܬܐ.. ܘܦܿܢܝ ܐܥܒܼܪ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܕܘܒܪܐ ܐܪܥܢܝܐ ܘܥܦܪܢܐ:. ܠܘܬ ܕܘܒܪܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܒܠܢܐ ܘܫܡܿܝܢܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܡܪܓܫܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܛܒ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܼܚܣܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܢ ܘܐܟ̈ܣܢܝܐ ܥܒܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܢ ܕܡܬܒܪܢܫܝܢܝܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܡܥܕܪܢܝܬܐ ܘܕܡܛܠ ° ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܢܼܦܠܘ ܐܬܦܪ̈ܢܣܬ܇. ܘܣܒܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ° ܕܨܐܬܐ
as from a vine, Christ, the grape, mounted without seed and sprouted. Where are they then, upon hearing these words, the partisans of the folly of Eutyches? Will they not give their adherence even to the first of the patriarchs who says: It is from my race, my son, that you have mounted? Let us deride the foolishness of the imagination (φαντασία) and confess that the very holy body of Christ is of our essence (οὐσία); Mary, indeed, is of our race (γένος) as well as Jacob her first father, because we are formed from the same mud, as says the book of Job. He who created and formed came to restore and create anew not another creature, but that which had fallen and had undergone the corruption of sin, by means of the divine incarnation, when He threw Himself like a leaven into all the mass of the human race (γένος), that He became the second Adam, that He delivered us by His resurrection and that He made us pass from the mortal and earthly state to the incorruptible and heavenly life. How do you not feel then that you deprive us of the goods of this kind and that you render us strangers to this incarnation which is a help and which was ordained for those who have fallen? Do you estimate that our flesh contracts some impurity or some stain


[p. 299] {#po.8.p299}
ܡܕܡ ܐܘ ܛܘܠܫܐ ܛܐܫ ܒܝܣܪܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܢܢ ܒܐܠܗܐ ܡܠܬܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܟܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܚܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܳܝ ܕܡܟܢܐ ܕܬܛܡܐ ܘܬܨܐܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܚܝܒܠܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܐܝܟܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܚܬ ܥܩܝܒܝܬܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ܇ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܚܠܦܢܐܝܬ ܡܬܒܣܪ. ܘܒܬܘܠܘܬܐ ܕܐܡܐ ܐܦ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܝܠܕܐ: ܘܡܓܢܢܘܬܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ܇ ܘܪܚܝܩܘܬܐ ܕܙܪܥܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ܇. ܘܟܠܗ ܛܘܠܫܐ ܘܠܐ ܫܟܝܚܘܬܐ ܘܓܠܝܙܘܬܐ ܕܪܓܬܐ. ܠܡܢܐ ܟܕ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܠܒܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ:. ܗܿܢܝܢ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܕܟܝܬܐ ܘܪܘܪ̈ܒܬܐܼ ܘܕܡܠܝܢ ܒܣܣܝܡܘܬ ܪܝܚܐ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ:. ܒܕܘܒܝܐ ܚܙܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܕܗܦܟܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡܢ ܐܦܝ̈ܟܘܢ ܡܢ ܗܿܢܝܢ ܕܡܟ̈ܣܡܢ ܘܡܟܢ̈ܢ ܕܢܗܢܝܢ ܠܣܘܩܐ. ܠܘܬ ܕܝܢ ܣܝܢܐ ܣܪܝܐ ܕܚܘܫܝ̈ܒܝܟܘܢ ܢܚܬܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܕܝܠܢ. ܐܢ ܓܝܪ ܛܡܐܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܝܣܪܐ ܕܝܠܢ.. ܡܝܕܝܢ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܡܢ ܫܘܪܝܐ ܙܕܩ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܓܒܠܝܘܗܝ ܠܗܢܐ܇ ܗܽܘ ܕܠܟܠܗܝܢ ܝܒܕ ܦܚܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܛܒ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܕܣܝܓܝ ܫܦܝܪ̈ܢ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܟܝܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܝ ܓܒܝܠܬܐ: ܘܡܕܡ ܚܘܣܪܢܐ ܐܘ ܛܡܐܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܐܚܬܝܬ ܠܗܽܘ ܕܓܒܠܗܿܢ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܘ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܕܟܝܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܓܒܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ.. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܕܒܟܡܐ ܡܨܥܝܘܬܐ ܘܪܘܚܩܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܕܐ ܦܐܝܬ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ. ܗܝ ܕܒܐܝܕ̈ܝܗ̇ ܗܽܘ ܗܽܘ ܕܓܒܠ ܨܒܝܐ ܕܢܬܓܒܠ ܒܒܣܪ. ܐܠܐ ܚܙܘܢ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܐܦ ܒܗܕܐ.. ܠܘܬ ܫܘ̈ܥܝܬܐ ܢܕܝܕ̈ܬܐ
in God the Word, without understanding that there is only one thing capable of rendering impure and dirty, namely the corruption of sin? And where there is a trace of sin, there where God the Word became incarnate without change, where the mother is a virgin even after childbirth, where the Holy Spirit descends, where the virile seed is absent, and where concupiscence is entirely excluded and absent and is lacking? Why, making abstraction of all these pure, great, and filled with a good divine odor circumstances, do you turn away like pigs from what is sweet and capable of pleasing the sense of smell, and descend into the foul mire of your thoughts, - I do not say, of our nature? For if our flesh is impure, it should not therefore have been formed, even from the beginning, by Him who made all things beautiful and good and very beautiful? But if this formation is pure and has caused to Him who formed it neither damage nor stain, how is the second formation not purer and is there not reason to say that, by the great distance that exists between them, this formation by which He who formed wanted Himself to be formed in the flesh, is more worthy of God than the first?


[p. 300] {#po.8.p300}
ܕܡܢ̈ܝܢܝܐ ܬܦܝܩܘܢ܇. ܘܟܕ ܠܦܓܪܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܐܝܟ ܡܨܐܐ ܘܛܡܐܐ ܠܟܝܪ ܡܢ ܪܐܙܐ ܣܝܡܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܒܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ:. ܐܦ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܬܩܕܡܘܢ ܝܗ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܘܠܦܢܛܐܣܝܐ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܚܠܨܬܘܢܗ̇. ܡܐܠܠܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܐܬܚܠܨܬܘܢ ܘܐܬܓܢܒܬܘܢ:. ܟܢ ܣܓܝ ܡܬܚܢܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܟܘܢ ܒܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ܇. ܘܟܕ ܓܢܒܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܟܕ ܡܬܓܢܒܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡܗܝܡܢܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܗܽܘ ܕܐܡܪ ܡܛܠ ܥܪ̈ܒܚܐ ܕܝܠܗ: ܕܓܢܒܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܐ: ܐܠܐ ܕܢܓܢܒ ܘܢܩܛܘܠ ܘܢܘܒܕ: ܐܢܐ ܐܬܝܬ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܝܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܝܬܝܪܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܦ ܠܟܘܢ ܢܫܟܚ ܘܢܦܢܝܟܘܢ ܕܐܬܓܢܒܬܘܢ
But take care not to precipitate us in this very way into the absurd fables of the Manichaeans and, by putting our body outside the mystery as stained and impure, to establish for it also another creator, as they do themselves. Indeed you have removed from there the imagination (φαντασία), or rather (μᾶλλον δέ) you have been stripped and despoiled of it, and you are well worthy of pity for the double motive that you steal and that you are stolen. As for us, we believe that He who said concerning His sheep: The thief comes only to steal, kill, and destroy; I have come, so that they may have life and that they may be in abundance, can equally make you re-enter into your goods, because you have been stolen and you have lost, and make you live, because you have been already the victims of incredulity.
ܘܐܒܕܬܘܢ.. ܘܢܚܝܟܘܢ ܕܐܬܢܟܣܬܘܢ ܡܢ ܟܕܘ ܒܐܝܕܝ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ܀ ܐܦ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܕܝܢ: ܙܒܢܐ ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܦܢܐ ܒܣܩܝܗ̇ ܠܡܠܬܐ ܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ ܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܢܦܩܬ: ܕܢܩܦܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܥܩܘܒ ܕܐܡܪ ܕܡܢ ܡܘܥܝܬܐ ܒܪܝ ܣܕܠܩܬ: ܠܓܠܛܝܐ ܟܬܒ ܟܕ ܐܡܪ.. ܕܐܡܬܝ ܕܝܓ ܕܐܬܐ ܡܘܠܝܗ ܕܙܒܢܝܐ. ܫܕܪ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܒܪܗ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ.. ܕܢܗܘܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܢܡܘܣܐ. ܗܿܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ.. ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܕܐܘܣܝܐ ܕܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܐܬܝܠܕ ܒܒܣܪ ܥܡܢܘܐܝܠ ܡܚܘܝܐ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܪ
Paul for his part - it is time indeed that we take up again the subject at the place where we left it, in order to show that he is in agreement with the word of Jacob: It is from my race, my son, that you have mounted, - wrote to the Galatians in these terms: When the times were fulfilled, God sent His Son so that He might be of a woman, so that He might be under the Law (νόμος). This word "of a woman" shows that the Emmanuel was born in the flesh of the essence (οὐσία) of the Virgin. He did not say "by a woman", in order (not) to give to those


[p. 301] {#po.8.p301}
ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ:. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܬܠ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܝܕܫܐܝܬ ܡܬܪܥܝܢ:. ܕܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܢܐܡܪܘܢܝܗܝ ܠܝܠܕܗ ܡܬܠܢܐܝܬ܇. ܘܕܐܝܟ ܕܒܣܝܠܘܢܐ ܪܗܛ܆. ܐܘ ܕܐܝܟ ܒܪܩܚܐ ܥܒܪ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ܂. ܐܦ ܒܛܝܢܐ ܡܫܡܠܝܐ ܡܬܢܣܒ ܠܘܬ ܡܬܚܙܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܬܒܝܣܪܢܘܬܐ ܫܪܝܪܬܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܦܢܛܝܐܣܝ. ܐܬܡܠܝܘ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܪ ܝܘܡܬܗ̇
who have bad thoughts the occasion to call His birth a passage in an allegorical way and to affirm that He ran as in a canal and that He passed like a lightning bolt. That is why one understands that there was a complete conception, in order to show that the incarnation is true and without imagination (φαντασία): The days indeed were fulfilled for her to give birth.
ܕܬܐܠܕ܀ ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܝܬܝܪ ܥܡܝܩܐܝܬ ܚܐܪ ܐܢܐ ܒܟܬܝ̈ܒܬܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܬܐ.. ܕܪܐܙܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܪܒ ܡܫܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܕܡܕܪܟ ܡܢܗܝܢ. ܡܛܠ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܕܡ ܡܢ ܡܢ ܚܘܝܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܛܥܝ: ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܐܬܛܥܝܬ: ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܂. ܟܕ ܠܗܢܐ ܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܨܒܐ ܕܢܐܣܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܠܬܐ.. ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܝܩܪܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܪܒ. ܐܠܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܡܐܣܐ ܐܘ ܡܬܪܨ.. ܠܘ ܠܗܝ ܪܝܫܝܬܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܒܥܐ ܡܐܝܬܐ ܠܗ ܠܗܘ ܕܟܪܝܗܢ. ܐܠܐ܂ ܐܦ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܗܘ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܡܘܣܦ ܠܗ. ܗܝ ܟܝܬ܂ ܕܐܦ ܠܘܬܢ ܕܝܠܢ ܥܒܕ. ܟܕ ܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܢ ܚܘܝܒܐ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܘܡܢ ܚܒܠܐ ܐܓܕܗܝ ܠܢ ܒܝܕ ܩܝܡܬܗ. ܐܠܐ܂ ܘܟܕ ܚܠܦ ܦܪܕܝܣܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܠܢ ܘܒ̈ܢܝܐ ܚܠܦ ܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܢ:. ܘܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ܀
As for me, considering more deeply the holy Scriptures, I find myself observing another greater mystery. Indeed because Adam had not been deceived by the serpent, but that the woman, once deceived, had been the first in the transgression, God the Word who wanted to heal this transgression, raised the woman to a greater honor. For when God heals or corrects, He does not bring only to the sick the principal, but He grants him in addition even the wealth that he did not have. That is what He did also for ourselves, not only by delivering us by His resurrection from the condemnation to death and from corruption, but also by leading us to the kingdom of heaven in place of paradise and by making us sons instead of slaves, and heirs of God.


[p. 302] {#po.8.p302}
ܡܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܙܘܘܓܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܪܒ܆. ܗܘ ܕܠܗ ܐܦܝܣܝܗ̇ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ. ܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ. ܦܘܠܘܣ ܟܕ ܪܝܫܢܘܬܐ ܘܛܟܣܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܝܗܒ ܠܓܒܪܐ!': ܘܠܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢܝܬܐ ܦܠܓ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ: ° ܘܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܐܡܪ ܕܙܕܩ ܠܗ̇ ܕܬܬܟܫܦ ܘܬܫܬܥܒܕ. ܐܡܪ.. ܕܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܓܒܪܐ ܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܐܠܐ܂ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܢ ܓܒܪܐ. ܐܬܒܪܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܡܢ ܐܠܥܗ ܕܐܕܡ. ܐܡܬܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܩܦܐ ܕܙܘܘܓܐ ̇ ܘܙܢܐ ܗܘ ܕܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܐܬܚܠܛ ܥܠ ܗܘܝܐ ܕܝܠܢ.. ܗܘܐ ܐܦ ܓܒܪܐ ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ܆. ܕܡܢ ܗܪܟܐ ܟܕ ܐܝܟ ܗܘ ܕܠܘܬ ° ܪܚܝܡܘܬܐ ܘܐܘܝܘܬܐ ܡܟܢܦ ܠܗܘܢ ܗܘ ܫܠܝܚܐ: ܠܘܬ ܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܐܚܕܘܢ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܩܫܝܐܝܬ ܥܠ ܢܫ̈ܐ܂. ܐܚܬܝ ܒܬܪܟܢ.. ܒܪܡ ܘܠܐ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܓܒܪܐ.. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܓܒܪܐ ܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܒܡܪܝܐ. ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܢ ܓܒܪܐ.. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܓܒܪܐ ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܚܢܢ ܡܢ. ܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܡܬܐܡܪܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܗܘܝܢܢ ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܘܠܘ ܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܪܡܝܗ ܓܝܪ ܕܙܪܥܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ ܩܕܝܡ. ܘܡܢܗ ܡܐܠܠܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܢ. ܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܕܠܐ ܙܪܥܐ ܐܬܒܛܢ.. ܠܘ ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ.. ܐܠܐ
What is then this greater honor, of which He judged the woman worthy? I am going to say it clearly. When Paul gives to man the authority and the first place (τάξις) and that he assigns to the woman the second rank, and that for this reason he says that she must be modest and submissive, he added: Man indeed is not drawn from the woman, but the woman is drawn from the man for she was created with a rib of the man. But as our existence depends on the union of marriage and the mode of carnal commerce, man also existed by the woman: from there comes that the Apostle himself, inviting them, as well as to love and to good understanding, to this that men do not exercise with hardness their authority with regard to women, added then: However the woman is not without the man, nor the man without the woman, in the Lord. For just as the woman was drawn from the man, likewise man exists also by the woman. But it is with good reason that we are said to have existed "by the woman" and not "of the woman", for (for us) the projection of the virile seed precedes and it is by it above all (μᾶλλον) that we are. Christ, on the contrary, conceived without seed, existed not "by the woman", but "of the woman" properly speaking, and He made her mount up to a rank (τάξις) which possesses superiority. Indeed in the
ܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܪܢܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ܂. ܘܠܛܟܣܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܪܝܫܢܘܬܐ ܐܣܝܩܗ̇. ܒܓܒܝܠܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܓܝܪ ܗܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ܂. ܡܢ ܐܕܡ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܒܓܒܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ. ܡܫܝܚܐ ܗܘ ܐܕܡ ܬܪܝܢܐ܂ ܡܢܗ̇. ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܕܝܢ ܡܝܬܪܐ ܗܕܐ
first formation the woman is of Adam and in the second formation Christ, the second Adam, is of the (woman), and this outweighs that as much as the second (formation) is more divine than the first.


[p. 303] {#po.8.p303}
ܡܢ ܗܝ܂. ܟܡܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܓܒܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܓܒܝܠܘܬܐ܀ ܟܕ ܗܟܝܠ ° ܠܕܐܝܟ ܗܢܐ ܐܝܩܪܐ ܐܬܬܘܝܬܝܢ ܐܘ ܢܫ̈ܐ: ܘܟܠܗܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܩܕܡ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܪ̈ܝܫܝܬܐ: ܒܝܕ ܝܠܕܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ.. ܡܠܟܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܛܒܝܢ ܘܕܠܘܬ ܚܝܐ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܡܡܠܝܢ ܡܠܟܝܢ ܠܓܒܪ̈ܝܟܝܢ. ܘܐܢܗܘ ܡܢ ܕܬܫܟܚܢ ܐܢܘܢ: ܕܩܕܡ̈ܝܐ ܠܒܕܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܙܕܩ̈ܢ: ܘܠܟܝܢ ܗܢܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܢܝܢ ܡܐܠܦܝܢ!'. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡ̈ܠܦܢܐ ܘܕܐܚܝܕܝܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܪܝܫܐ. ܐܙܥܩܝܢ ܐܕܢܟܝܢ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܗܝ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ: ܕܒܪ̈ܓܝܓܬܐ ܐܣܝܪܝܢ ܐܘ ܒܡܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܥܠܡ̈ܢܝܬܐ: ܘܕܡܬܚܢܩܝܢ ܡܛܠ ܟܘܢܫܐ ܕܕܗܒܐ: ܘܕܠܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܢܬܐ ܢܦܩܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ: ܘܡܬܪܢܝܢ ܕܠܡܢܘ ܡܢ ܡܢ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܚܝܒܝܢ ܒܒܝܬ ܐܣܝܪ̈ܐ ܢܚܒܫܘܢ: ܠܡܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܦܟܝܪ ܢܓܕܘܢ ܢܝܬܘܢ ܠܒܝܬ ܕܝܢܐ: ܥܠ ܒܝܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢܘ ܢܦܠܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܡܫܥܒܕ ܬܚܝܬ ܚܘܒܬܐ: ܘܙܟܐ ܘܡܛܠܩ ܒܣܓܝܐܘܬܗܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܒܝܬܐ: ܠܡܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܢܗ ܕܠܒܘܫܐ ܗܘ ܐܠܨܝܐ ܢܫܠܚܘܢ.. ܠܐ ܬܫܗܪ̈ܢ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܥܗܪܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܠܐ ܕܘܘܢܐ ܘܚܪܡܐ܂. ܐܦܠܐ ܒܡ̈ܠܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܠܩܐܟܚܝܢ ܐܟܪܢܝܐ
Since you have been judged worthy, O women, of such an honor, of what is the first and the principal, by the intermediary of the Virgin Mother of God, give then to your husbands very good counsels that lead them to the future life. If you find that they are the first to do their duty and to teach it to you, lend them your ear as to masters who occupy the first place (τάξις). If on the contrary you find that they are bound by the desires and the worries of the world, that they are suffocated by the thought of gathering gold, that they pass the nights without sleeping and that they ask themselves with anxiety what debtor they are going to put in prison, what prisoner they will drag and cite before the tribunal, on the house of what man they will fall under the pretext that he is under the weight of a debt and that he is surpassed and devoured by the great number of interests, what being they will strip even of the necessary clothing, do not pass with them a vigil thus full of misery and of anathema and do not activate by your words as well as


[p. 304] {#po.8.p304}
ܕܒܪܘܚܐ܆. ܠܫܠܗ̇ܒܝܬܐ ܕܪܚܡܼܬ ܟܣܦܐ ܬܢܨܒܪ̈ܝܢ. ܒܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܕܡ ܪܚܡ ܐܠܗܐ: ܘܒܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܓܒܪܢܝܬܐ݀ ܡܠܐ ܚܟܝ̈ܡܬܐ ܢܨܝ̈ܠܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ.. ܘܠܝܬܝܪܬܐ ܕܝܬܝܪܘܬܐ' ܗܿܝ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܕܥܘܟܝܢ. ܘܠܘܬ ܒܪ ܙܘܓܗܿ ܟܠ ܚܕܐ ܡܢܟܝܢ ܬܐܡܪ. ܝܗܼܒ ܠܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܘܪܟܬܐ ܕܣܦܩܐ ܠܢ: ° ܒܫܝܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܒܣܝܡܘܬܐ'° ܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܡܬܒܣܡܝܢ ܚܢܢ. ܡܢ ܟܠ ܕܐܬܪ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ° ܠܢ ܛܒ̈ܝܬܐ. ܡܢ ܥܲܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܝܠܟ ܒܣܡܝܢ ܚܢܢ.. ܗܳܢܘܢ ܕܒܪܟ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܬܘ̈ܣܦܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢܗ.. ܗܽܘ ܝܗܘܒܐ ܕܛܒ̈ܬܐ. ܘܐܡܬܝ ܕܬ݂ܚܙܝܢܝܗܝ ܕܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܟܢ ܐܸܬܪܟܟ ܘܐܼܬܦܨܚ: ܘܩܠܝܠ ܐܫܦ ܘܐܸܬ݀ܪܦܝ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܚܝܼܨܘܬܐ ܕܥܣܩܐ ܡܪܝܪܬܐ܂. ܡܚܕܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܐܝܬܝ ° ܒܬܪܟܢ.. ܢܬܚܫܒ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ̄ ܪܚܘܡܝ܂. ܐܦ ܡܕܡ ܪܚܡ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܛܠ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܣܟܢܝܢ. ܬܒ̈ܥܬܐ ܕܚܘܒ̈ܬܐ܂ ܠܘ ܗܟܢܐ ܢܥܼܒܕ ܐܢܝܢ ܚܝܼܨܐܝܬ 6ܐܙ). ܢܚܼܣܒ ܡܢܝܬܐ. ܠܦܘܪܥܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܘ ܕܫܪܟܐ܂. ܐܘ ܢܓܪ ܩܠܝܠ.. ܐܘ ܢܫܒܘܩܝܗܝ ܡܣܟܢܝܢ ܒܫܒܘܩ. ܚܢܢ ܕܡ ܚܢܢ. ܗܿܢܘܢ܂ ܡܕܡܥܝܢ. ܚܢܢ ܣܐܒܥܝܢ ܚܢܢ.. ܘܡܢ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܡܬܒܣܡܝܢ ܚܢܢ. ܠܼܚܡܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܟܠܝܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ܂. ܘܟܠܗ ܟܒܪ
by the wind the flame of the love of money. But in a thought inspired by the love of God and in a courageous spirit, make wise words resound for them and extinguish in them the desire for the abundance of (riches) alone. Let each of you say to her husband: "God has given us a blessing sufficient for us and we enjoy the peace and the happiness from above; from every side goods arrive to us: we rejoice in the fact of your labors, which He who gives the goods has blessed by adding His own." When you see that he has become gentle and cheerful by words of this kind, that he has shown himself benevolent and that he has relaxed from the violence of his cruel hardness, add immediately after: "O my friend, show then some charitable sentiments for the unfortunate; do not claim with such exactitude the payment of debts; receive a part, and delay a little or even abandon the payment of the rest, if they are deeply unfortunate. We, we are in joy, and they, they are in tears; we, we are satiated and we enjoy the superfluous, and they, they have only bread, perhaps it is measured to them or even it is lacking to them following the degree of the need. On my side I will bring also something at the same time as the effusion of your kindness and I will not torment you by
ܕܡܡܿܫܚ.. ܐܘ ܕܐܦ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܡܝ̣ܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܣܘܢܩܢܐ ܚܣܝܪ. ܡܥܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܟܚܕܐ݀ ܥܡ ܫܦܝܥܘܬ ܛܒܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܟ. ܘܠܐ ܝܩܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܒܡܲܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܕܟܢܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܫܿܒܩܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܗܘ ܠܒܘܫܐ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܝܬ ܕܬܥܼܒܕ ܠܝ ܘܗܠܝܢ ܚܫ̈ܠܬܐ ܕܕܗܒܐ܂. ܘܢܦܩܬܐ ܗܿܝܿ ܕܥܠ ܨܒܬܐ ܗܘ ܐ̱ܚܪܢܐ. ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܬܙܒܢ ܠܝ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ. ܢܚ̈ܬܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ' ܕܣܚܼܡܝܢ ܠܓܘ ܒܠܚܘܕܝܗܘܢܼ܇ ܣܦܩܝܢ ܠܝ. ܡܪܚܡܢܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܥܠ ܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ ܡܨܒܬܐ ܠܝ ܝܬܝܪ ܟܢܝܟܐܝܬ.. ܛܒ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܢܚ̈ܬܐ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܝ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܬܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܦܐܝܘܬܐ: ܗܿܝ ܕܠܐ ܣܐܒܐ݀ ° ܘܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܪܚܼܝܡܐ.. ܠܘ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܢ ܠܒܪ. ܗܺܝ ܕܚܝ̈ܡܝܐ” ܘܢܦܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܗܒܒܐ. ܟܝܣܐ ܛܒܐ ܘܿܠܐ ܡܬܚܠܨܢܐ.. ܡܢ ܗܪܟܐ ܐܣܚܡ ܠܒܢ̈ܝ. ܟܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܦܝܠܘܣܘܦܝܝܣܐ ܠܥܝܕܐ ܐܢܬܝ܂. ܟܕ ܡܥ ܓܒܪܟܝ.. ܐܦܢ ܢܗܘܐ܂ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܕܡܝܼܘܬ ܚܝܘܬܐ ܫ̱ܢܐ܂. ܡܢ ܟܠ ܦܪܘܣ ܢܫܚܠܦ ܬܪܥܝܬܗܢ. ܘܪܚܝܡ ܐܢܫܐ' ܢܗܘܐ ܠܚܝ̈ܒܐ. ܘܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܓܝܪ ܗܟܢܐ ܣܦܩ ܕܠܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܢܦܝܣ܆.. ܐܝܟ ܢܫ̈ܐ ܟܢܝ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܢܟ̈ܦܬܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܐܢ ܢܗܘܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܟܠܗ ܡܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܘܨܢܝܥܘܬܐ ܕܪܝܛܘܪ̈ܐܣ4. ܟܢ ܡܧ̈ܟܚܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ ܗܘܟܝܚܝܠ ܕܐܥܒܕ̈ܢ ܛܝܿܒܬܐ݀ ܐܸܢ ܠܐ ܬܥܒܕ̈ܢ.. ܠܟܬܒܐ ܫ̈ܡܥܝܢ ܕܐܡܿܪ. ܕܚܛܼܝܬܐ ܬܬܚܫܝܒ ܠܟܝܢ. ܕܝܕܥ ܓܝܪ ܐܸܡܿܪ ܛܒܬܐ ܠܡܥܒܕ ܘܠܐ ܠܒܕ.. ܚܛܗܐ ܐܝܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ܀
the worries of this year: I abandon to you this clothing that you were to make for me as well as these jewels of gold and this sum for this other ornament; you will buy me absolutely nothing; the clothes that one wears inside only are enough for me; mercy toward the indigent provides me a very honest clothing, which is much more so than all the clothes of great price. Inside I will have for beauty that which does not age and which is agreeable to God, and not that which is exterior, withers and falls like the flower. By that I will constitute for my children a precious and impregnable purse." When your husband hears you hold these reasonings, were he similar to a wild beast, he will necessarily change his dispositions and he will become charitable toward his debtors. For no one is capable of persuading men thus as honest and pure women, even if one had all the eloquence and all the skill of the rhetoricians. Consequently if you do not do good when you can do it, listen to the Scripture which says that this sin will be imputed to you: He who knows the good that must be done, it says indeed, and does not do it, renders himself guilty of sin.


[p. 305] {#po.8.p305}


[p. 306] {#po.8.p306}
ܐܦ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܗܢܝܐܘܐ ܚܫܐ! ܬܚܙܝ̈ܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܚܦܛܝܢ ܠܟܝܢ: ܘܠܘܬ° ܐܘܪ̈ܚܬܐ ܣܒܝܣ̈ܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܕܦܝܿܢܐ': ܘܿܠܘܬ ܟܘܫܐ ܪܛܝܒܐ ܘܕܢܦܩܬܐ ܕܬܡܢ: ܐܝܟܢܐ ° ܕܗܟܢܐ ܟܢܝܟܐܝܬ ܢܐܡܪܝܗܿܢ. ܟܕ ܥܠ ܝܠܕܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܩ̈ܝܡܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ ܗܘܢܟܝܢ: ܐܝܡ ܕܥܠ ܦܢܩܝܬܐ ܕܝܘ̈ܠܦܢܐ ܟܗ̈ܢܝܐ: ܘܡܢܗ݀ ܬܪܥܝܬܐ° ܕܟܼܝܬܐ ܡܥܡܕ̈ܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ ܒܟܝܢ.. ܙ̈ܕܚܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܘܬ ܛܒܬܐ ܘܐܠܲܦܝ̈ܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܕ ܐܡ̇ܪܲܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ.. ܒܟܢܫܘܬܐ ܘܢܟܦܘܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܐܘ̄ ܓܒܪܝ ܢܝܩܪ ܚ̈ܝܐ ܕܝܠܢ. ܒܟܘ̈ܫܐ ܘܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܢܟ̈ܦܐ. ܕܒܒܝܬܢ.. ܢܥܒܕܝܘܗܝ ܠܙܘܘܓܐ܂ ° ܪܐܙܐ. ܘܠܥܪܣܐ° ܕܟܝܬܐ. ܢܗ̈ܘܝܢ ܠܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܚܘܠܛܝܢܐ. ܠܘ ܡܥܩܒܢܘܬܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܝܨܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܓܝܓܬܐ.. ܐܠܐ ܚܢܘܬܐ ܪܐܙܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠܢܝܬܐ ܘܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܬܚܕܗܒܬ ܕܡܘܠܕܘܬܐ ܒܢ̈ܝܐ. ܒܗܠܝܢ ܡܠܐ: ܬܦܓ̈ܕܢ ܡܿܢ ܠܚܝܐܦܐ ܫܟܝܖ̈ܐ ܕܓܒܪ̈ܝܟܝܢ܀ ܕܒܩܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܟܦܐ ܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ ܘܚܕܝܢ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܐܚܬܝܗܘܢ ܠܟܝܢ: ܘܗܼܝ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܛܒܬܢܝܬܐ: ܟܠܗ̇ ܓܘܕܐ ܕܩ̈ܕܝܫܐ.܂. ܕܡܟܠܠ ܠܟܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢܐܝܬ:. ܗܘ ܣܐ݀ܡ ܐܓܘܢܝܐ ܕܡܝܬܪܘܬܐ.. ܗܽܘ ܕܡܢ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܘܠܘ ܒܝܕ ܐܢܬܬܐ܇ ܘܡܢ ܗܺܝ ܠܡܫܪܪܘܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܘܚܝܠܝܡܘܬܐ ܢܕܒ ܒܟܝܢ܀
Likewise when you see your husbands push you toward the voluptuousness of passion, toward the frequented paths that lead to Daphne toward the humid and expensive life of that place, to give by that an honest name to it, fix your intelligence on the Mother of God as well as on the book of holy doctrines and, after having drawn from it a pure spirit, make it inhabit in you. Push your husbands toward the good and give them a lesson by saying: Let us honor our life, O my husband, above all by honesty and purity; by the pure life and conduct of our house let us make of marriage a mystery, and of the bed a pure (place); let the affairs of carnal commerce be for us not a question and a search for evil desires, but a mysterious, ineffable habitation given by God in view of the production of children. By these words you will put a brake on the senseless violences of your husbands, and moreover the angels will applaud you and will rejoice concerning you as well as the blessed Virgin and all the army of the saints, and Christ will crown you in an invisible manner, He who delivers the combat (ἀγών) of virtue, who existed "of the woman" and not "by the woman" and who by her has put in you this firmness and this courage.


[p. 307] {#po.8.p307}
ܐܦ ܡܛܠ ܬܪܒܝܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܒ̈ܢܝܟܘܢ ܕܝܠܟܘܢ.܆ ܠܐ ܬܫܚܦܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܓܒܪ̈ܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ ܡܘܒܠܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ: ܘܠܘܬ ܣܘܓܦܢܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܢܐ ܐܘ ܚܝܒܠܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ܂. ܠܘܬ ܥܕܬܐ ܐܬܠܝܢ ܒܗܘܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ. ܠܘܩܒܠ ܦܘܪܢܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܘܒܕܢܐ.. ܠܥܝܕܐ ܗܿܘ ܝܬܝܪ ܢܨܝܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܝܫ̈ܐ ܐܩܝܡܝܢ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܬܓܢܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܬܓܪܓܝܢ: ܟܕ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܐܦ ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ ܐܡܪܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ.. ܕܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܥܕܥܕ ܫܦܝܪ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܙܘܢܐ ܐܙܠ܆. ܐܘ ܠܘܬ ܣܘܥܪܢܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܕܕܡܿܐ ܕܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܢܡܘܣܐ. ܘܟܕ ܗܟܢܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܥܝ̈ܕܐ ܘܙܢ̈ܝܐ ܡܬܪܒܝܢ ܘܥܡܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܘܠܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܩܘܡܬܐ ܐܬܝܢ: ܠܘ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ: ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܕܡܝܬܪܘܬܐ܂. ܐܝܟ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܟܕܘ ܐܬܒܝܬܝܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܚܕܐ ܒܢܝܢܫܐ ܠܗܽܘ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܫܒܘܩܘ ܛܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܬܐܟܠܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܐܬܘܢ ܠܘܬܝ܆. ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ܇ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܠܟܘܢ ܚܘܛܪܐ ܕܣܝܒܘܬܐ܆:. ܘܒܘܝܐܐ ܕܟܠ ܥܩܐ. ܢܬܪܕܘܢ ܓܝܪ ܥܡ ܗܠܝܢ
Likewise, regarding the education of your children, do not be condescending toward your husbands. But when they lead them to performances and to anything else that wounds and corrupts the soul, apply yourselves to leading them to church; oppose the pernicious voluptuousness with the most brilliant morals of the saints; touch them vividly and urge them, saying to them at the same time in a firm manner: 'He cannot celebrate the feasts of God as he ought, who goes to performances and to any other business of the same kind that is found outside the Law (νόμος).' When in this way they have been raised in these morals and habits, when they have grown in them and have reached the measure of age not only of time, but also of virtue, like those who have united once and for all * with Him who said: 'Let the little children come to me and do not hinder them, for to those who resemble them belongs the kingdom of heaven,' they will be for you a staff of old age and a consolation for every anguish. For they will be instructed by these other words: 'Honor your father and your mother, * so that you may be happy and prolong your life on this earth.'
ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ܂. ܐܦ ܗܿܝ ܕܚܩܪ ܠܐܒܘܟ ܘܠܐܡܟ.܇. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܫܦܝܪ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܟ ܘܬܗܘܐ ܢܓܝܪ ܙܒܢܐ ܥܠ ܐܪܥܐ܀ ܚܙܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܠܘ ܣܪܝܩܐܝܬ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܝܬ ܟܕ ܡܫܪܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܡܐܡܪܐ: ܕܐܢ
You see that it is not in vain that I said at the beginning of


[p. 308] {#po.8.p308}
ܢܣܒ ܦܪܕܬܐ ܙܥܘܪܬܐ ܕܣܘܟܠܐ̇ ܡܢ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܚܟܝܡܬܐ ܐܟܙܢܝܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܛܠܝܘܢ: ܘܠܗܕܐ ܢܪܬܚ: ܢܫܬܡܠܐ ܡܢܗ̇ ܡܐܢܐ ܪܒܐ܂. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܦܬܝܐ ܢܦܝܩܢܢ.. ܢܘܛܝܦܬܐ ܕܡ̈ܠܐ ܐܘ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܠܐ ܐܫܟܚܢܢ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܒܝܕܘܒ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܝܘܒ܀
this homily that, if we took a small seed of thought in the abundance of wisdom as in a mine (μέταλλον) and heated it, we would make it a great object; for behold, after having extended ourselves so lengthily, we have not found a drop of his words or his thoughts, as Job says in one place.
ܨܒܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܟܝܠ ܕܠܗ ܠܡܐܢܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܣܓܝ ܡܥܠܝܐ ܢܥܒܕܝܘܗܝ: ܘܒܗܢܐ ܒܕܡܝܘܬ ܟܐܦܝ̈ܐ ܡܝܩܪ̈ܬܐ: ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܡܦܬܟܐ ܘܥܡ̈ܝܩܐ ܢܚܪܘܙ.. ܐܠܐ ܢܫܒܘܩ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܦܪܘܣ ܝܘܡܐ. ܘܙܕܩ ܕܥܡ ܙܒܢܐ ܢܡܫܚܝܗ ܠܪܒܩܐܗ ܕܡܐܡܪܐ̇. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܕܢܕܥ ܙܒܢܐ ܠܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܡܬܦܩܕܝܢܢ. ܒܪܡ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܡܐ ܘܢܫܒܩܝܘܗܝ ܠܡܐܢܐ ܕܟܠ ܟܠܗ ܠܐ ܡܨܒܬܐ.. ܢܒܗܕܪܝܘܗܝ ܠܗܢܐ ܒܫܪܝܐ
You want us, therefore, to make this object even more remarkable and to set into it, like precious stones, varied and profound thoughts; but the day would necessarily abandon us and we must regulate the length of the homily according to the time, because we are obliged to recognize the time in all things. However, so as not to abandon and not to leave this object totally devoid of ornaments, we are going to adorn it by resolving a single question.
ܕܚܕܐ ܒܥܝܬܐ܀ ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܝܠܕܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܢܚܬܐ ܒܚܝܘܒܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܕܝܗܘܕܐ ܘܡܢ ܕܘܝܕ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܓܒܪܝܐܝܠ.. ܘܗܐ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܐܚܝܢܬܟܝ ܒܛܝܢܐ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܕܙܟܪܝܐ ܟܗܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܗܕܐ ܐܢܬܬܐ܂. ܟܕ ܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܕܠܘ̈ܝܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܦܪܘܣ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܗܘܬ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܢܡܘܣܝܬܐ ܕܗܘ ܕܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ
Since the Virgin Mother of God descends by her family from the tribe of Judah and David, how did Gabriel * say to her: 'Behold, Elizabeth, your kinswoman, has conceived'? For the latter, being the wife of the priest Zechariah, necessarily belonged to the tribe of the Levites. Indeed, it was not in accordance with the Law that one who belonged to one tribe should take his wife from another


[p. 309] {#po.8.p309}
ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܢܣܒ ܠܗ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܐܡܪ ܓܝܪ ܒܡ̈ܢܝܢܐ ܡܪܝܐ ܒܝܕ ܡܘܫܐ,. ܟܠܚܝܢ ܒܝܪܬܘܬܐ ܕܫܒܝܛܐ ܕܐܒܝܘܗܝ. ܢܬܢܩܦܘܢ ܒܙܘܘܓܐ ܒܝ̈ܢܝ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ. ܘܟܠ ܒܪܬܐ ܕܡܚܝܒܐ ܒܝܪܬܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܫܒ̈ܛܐ ܕܒ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ.. ܠܚܕ ܡܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܫܪܒܬܐ ܕܐܒܝܘܗܝܢ ܢܗ̈ܘܝܢ ܢܫ̈ܐ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܟܝܬ: ܘܟܕ ܝܘܣܦ ܐܬܚܫܒ ܒܫܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬ ܥܝܕܐ ܠܟܬܒܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ: ܕܡܟܬܒܘܬ ܫܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܡܢ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܒܕ ܘܠܘ ܡܢ ܢܫ̈ܐ܂. ܕܐܬܚܫܒܬ ܒܟܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܡܪܝܡ ܡܣܬܒܪܐ.
tribe; for the Lord says through Moses in Numbers: 'The children of Israel shall contract marriage each in the inheritance of the tribe of his father; and every daughter, who by her inheritance shall be related to all the tribes of the children of Israel, shall be the wife of one of those who belong to the family of her father.' This is why when the genealogy of Joseph was drawn up, given that the divine Book is accustomed to establish the genealogy according to the men and not according to the women, one must have drawn up (at the same time) that of Mary, seeing that according to the Law (νόμος) she belongs to the same tribe as her fiancé.
ܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܫܒܛܐ ܕܡܟܝܪܗܿ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܢܡܘܣܐ܀ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܚܝܢܬܗ̇ ܕܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܝ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐܼ. ܐܢܫܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܟܝܠ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܝܠܝܦܘܬܐ ܐܡܪܘܢ. ܕܡܛܠ ܕܐܝܣܪ̈ܐܝܠܝܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܘܝ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ ܘܡܢ ܚܕ ܥܡܐ ܡܬܬܝ̈ܬܝܢ ܗܘܝ܂. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܒ̈ܢܬ ܓܢܣܐ ܩܪܐ ܐܢܝܢ ܓܒܪܝܐܝܠ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܡܠܬܐ: ܕܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢܗ ܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܕܥܡܐ ܒܪ ܓܢܣܐ ܢܩܪܐ ܐܢܫ.. ܐܡܬܝ ܕܟܕ ܡܛܠ ܐܢܫ ܐ̱ܚܪܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܡܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܡܿܠܠ.. ܠܩܐ ܦܘܪܫܐ ܕܗܢܐ:. ܠܗܘ ܕܡܢܗ ܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܕܓܢܣܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ܆. ܢܩܪܐ ܒܪ ܓܢܣܐ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܦܘܠܘܣ ܒܐܓܪܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܠܘܬ ܪ̈ܘܡܝܐ ܇̈܇ ܟܕ ܡܛܠ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ ܕܫܪܟܐ ܘܐܦ ܡܢ ܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܗܝܡܢܘ ܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܡܿܠܠ..
How then is the Holy Virgin the kinswoman of Elizabeth? Some have said out of ignorance that it is because both were Israelites and were issued from the same people * that Gabriel called them kinswomen. Now, one has a motive to call someone a kinsman who belongs to the same people, when one speaks of another person who belongs to another people * and that, to distinguish him from it, one calls kinsman the one who belongs to the same race (γένος); it is thus that Paul, in the letter to the Romans, being on the point of


[p. 310] {#po.8.p310}
ܥܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܒ̈ܢܝ ܓܢܣܗ ܟܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܕ ܐܸܡܿܪ܇ ܚܠܦ ܐܚܝ̈ܝ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܒ̈ܢܝ ܓܢܣܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܒܒܣܪ.. ܗܢܘܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܐܝܣܪ̈ܠܝܐ. ܕܐܝܟ ܗܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܕܡ.. ܘܠܐ ܒܕܘܟ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܘܡܛܠ ܡܪܝܡ ܢܫܟܚ.. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܟܕ ܢܫ̈ܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܝ ܡܢܗ ܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܕܥܡܐ.. ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܠܘܬ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܘܘܕܥܐ ܝܕܝܥܐ܇. ܗܿܝ ܕܗܐ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܐܚܝܢܬܟܝ. ܐܠܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܡܫܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܓܝ̈ܠܝܢܐ ܐܠܗ̈ܝܐ ܘܕܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ܂. ܠܘ ܦܫܝܛܐܝܬ! ܐܡ̣ܪܗܿ̇ ܠܗܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܠܘܬ ܝܘܣܦ݀ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܓܝܼܠܝ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܒܚܠܡܐ݀ ܐܸܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܂. ܝܘܣܦ ܒܪܗ ܕܕܘܝܕ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܢܒܣܩܝܗ̇ ܠܬܪܥܝܬܗ. ܗܽܘ ܕܒܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܡܠܟ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܡܢ ܕܘܝܕ ܢܗܘܐ ܡܫܝܚܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܦ ܗܪܟܐ.. ܗܺܝ ܕܗܐ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܐܚܝܢܬܟܝ܂. ܡܠܝܐܬ ܡܢ ܪܐܙܐ ܦܐܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܗܿܝ ܟܝܬ ܕܐܢܐ ܡܝܩܪܝܢ ܐܢܐ܂. ܕܐܦܠܐ ܠܗܿ ܠܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܛܥܬ ܗܘܬ ܟܕ ܒܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܡܫܘܬܦܐ ܗܘܬ
speaking of those who among the other nations and also among the Jews had believed, gives the Jews the name of kinsmen by saying: 'In place of my brothers who are my own kinsmen according to the flesh, who are Israelites.' But we will find nowhere anything similar about Elizabeth and Mary; and while many women belonged to the same people, the angel would not have said to the Virgin by way of a precise sign this word: 'Behold Elizabeth your kinswoman...' But as he was the minister of divine revelations and spiritual orders, he did not pronounce this word in a general manner. Indeed, the angel who appeared to Joseph in a dream said to him: 'Joseph, son of David,' in order to remind him of the promise by which God had promised that Christ would be issued from David. It is therefore the same here, and this word: 'Behold Elizabeth your kinswoman...' is filled with a mystery worthy of God. In my opinion, this had not escaped the Virgin either, because she participated in the Holy Spirit; that is why * she submitted herself cheerfully and ran immediately to Elizabeth.
ܡܛܠܗܕܐ݀ ° ܐܦ ܚܕܝܐܝܬ ܡܫܬܡܥܐ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܚܕܐ ܒܪܫܥܬܗ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܡܣܬܪܗܒܐ ܗܘܬ܀ ܡܛܠ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ݀ ܐܦ ܡܿܠܟܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܕܡܡܠܟܝܢ°: ܒܗ̇ܝ
Because Christ was the king of kings in that he is God and Lord of the universe, and because he was furthermore called high priest after having become


[p. 311] {#po.8.p311}
ܟܝܬ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܡܪܝܐ ܕܟܠ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ: ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܐܦ ܪܝܫ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ ܐܬܬܣܝܡ܇ ܟܕ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܪܢܫܐ: ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܠܗܿ ܩܪܒ ܕܒܚܬܐ ܘܩܘܪܒܢܐ: ܕܘܟܝܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ: ܘܟܕ ܠܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܕܒܗ ܡܟܗܢ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܟܝܬ ܘܐܼܦ ܠܐܒܐ: ܐܬܒܩܘ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܿܪ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܒܫܠܝܚܐ ܘܪܝܫ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ ܕܬܘܕܝܬܢ ܝܫܘܥ. ܩܕܡܡܬ ܐܬܦܪܢܣܝܬ ܗܕܐ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ.. ܕܢܬܚܠܛ ° ܗܼܘ ܓܢܣܐ. ܕܫܒܛܐ ܡܠܟܝܐ ܕܝܗܘܕܐ. ܘܕܫܒܛܐ ܟܗܢܝܐ ܕܠܘܝ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܿܠܟܐ ܘܪܝܫ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ܂. ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܢܚܬܐ ܒܚܘܒܠܐ ܒܒܣܪ. ܟܬܝܒ ܓܝܪ ܒܡܦܩܢܐ.. ܕܩܕܡ ܕܢܬܬܣܝܡ ܝܚܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܟܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܡܣܒ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܐ̱ܚܪܢܐ.. ܕܐܗܪܘܢ ܗܽܘ ܪܝܫ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܢܡܘܣܐ.. ܢܣܒ ܡܢ ܫܒܛܐ ܕܝܗܘܕܐ ܐܢܬܬܐ.܂. ܠܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܒܪܬܐ ܕܥܡܝܢܐܕܒ. ܥܡܝܢܐܕܒ ܕܝܢ.. ܡܢ ܝܗܘܕܐ ܢܚܬ ܗܘܐ ܒܫܘܒܠܐ. ܘܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܢܫ: ܥܡܝܢܐܕܒ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܢܝܬܒܪ. ܟܕ ܟܠܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܛܘܥܝܝ: ܘܝܬܝܪ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܚ̈ܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܗܕܐ ܟܬܒܐ ܟܗܢܝܐ.. ܐܡܼܪ ܒܪܬܐ ܕܥܡܝܢܐܕܒ܂. ܚܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܚܫܘܢ. ܘܚܙܝ ܠܫ ܠܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܚܟܝܡܬ ܒܟܠ ܕܪܘܚܐ:. ܗܽܘ ܕܕܒܪ ܘܦܪܢܣ ܕܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܐܢܬܬܗ ܕܙܟܪܝܐ ܗܝ ܐܡܗ ܕܡܥܡܕܢܐ̇. ܗܿܝ ܒܪܬ ܓܢܣܐ ܕܒܠܕܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܪܝܡ܆. ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܬܬܟܢܐ:. ܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܗܝ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܡܣܩܐ ܠܢ ° ܗܳܝ ܕܐܗܪܘܢ ܢܣܒ ܗܘܐ:. ܘܗܝ ܕܒܐܦܕܝܗ̇ ܗܼܘܬ
man, in that he offered himself as a sacrifice and an offering to purify the sin of the world and in that he himself fulfills our confession toward him and toward the Father: 'Consider indeed,' says Paul, 'the apostle and high priest of our faith, Jesus.' It was decided from above that the race (γένος) of the royal tribe of Judah and that of the priestly tribe of Levi would be united, * in such a way that Christ, king and high priest, descended from these two tribes by his family in the flesh. It is written indeed in Exodus that, before the commandment was brought which forbade taking a wife from another tribe, Aaron, the first high priest according to the Law (νόμος), took a wife from the tribe of Judah: Elizabeth, daughter of Amminadab; and Amminadab descended by his family from the tribe of Judah. So that no one would go thinking of another Amminadab, the sacred Book, removing error and showing it very clearly, said: 'Daughter of Amminadab, sister of Nahshon.' See the particularly wise direction of the Spirit who directed and caused the wife of Zechariah, the mother of the Baptist, the kinswoman of Mary Mother of God, to be called Elizabeth, and who brings us back to this Elizabeth, * whom Aaron had


[p. 312] {#po.8.p312}
ܢܩܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܫܒ̈ܛܐ:. ܘܒܝܕ ܫܡܗ̇ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܙܥܩܐ. ܕܡܢ ܗܳܝ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܠܝ ܐܚܝܢܘܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܠܘܬ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ܀ ܘܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܐܡܪ ܠܝ܆. ܕܫܒܝܐ ܗܘ ܕܒܒ̈ܠܝܐ ܒܠܒܠܗ ܠܥܡܐ ܕܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܝܐ:. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܚܕ ܡܟܝܠ ܦܘܪܫܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܐܝܬ ܕܓܢ̈ܣܐ ܐܘ ܕܫܒ̈ܛܐ. ܡܝܘܕܥ ܓܝܪ ܥܙܪܐ.. ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܦܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܒܒܠ ܥܡܠܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܣܿܒܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ.. ܕܐܝܟ ܕܡܨܝܐ ܢܦܢܘܢ ܠܒ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܐܗܪܘܢ ܘܠܠܝܘܝ̈ܐ ܗܿܢܝܘܢ ܕܫܪܟܐ. ܒܛܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܓܝܪ ܥܠ ܥܝ̈ܕܐ ܐܒ̈ܗܝܐ ܕܝܠܝܗܘܢ. ܡܫܘܕܥ ܓܝܪ ܘܪܫܡ ܗܿܘ ܕܟܬܒ. ܠܘ ܒܚܠܚܘܕ ܠܫܒܛܐ ܕܗܿܢܝܘܢ ܕܦܢܝܘܢ. ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܐ܂ ܐܦ
married and by whom the union of the two tribes had taken place, and who proclaims to us clearly that it is through this Elizabeth that I must have the kinship with the Virgin. Let no one tell me that the captivity among the Babylonians threw confusion among the Jewish people, in such a way that there was since that time no distinction between the races (γένος) and the tribes. Ezra indeed declares that all those who returned from Babylon took great pains to gather as much as possible the children of Aaron and the Levites who remained; for they were concerned with the habits of their ancestors and the historian indicates and notes not only the tribe of those who returned, but also the cities and the villages.
ܠܩܘܙ̈ܝܣ܀ ܐܦ ܠܘܩܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܠܚܬܬܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܬܐܡܝܪ ܡܚܘܝܐ ܠܢ. ܐܡܝܪ ܡܛܠ ܙܟܪܝܐ ܟܕ ܐܡܪ. ܘܐܢܬܬܗ. ܡܢ ܒ̈ܢܬܐ ܕܐܗܪܘܢ. ܘܫܡܗܿ ܐܠܝܫܒܥ܀
Likewise Luke, showing us the exactitude of what has been said, speaks of Zechariah in these terms: 'His wife was from among the daughters of Aaron and was called Elizabeth.'
ܐܦ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܝܢ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܡܪ ܗܼܘ ܡܛܠ ܢܦܫܗ: ܕܦܐܐ ܠܢ ܕܢܡܠܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܟܐܢܘܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܬܬܝܬܘܬܐ ܚܝܕ̈ܢ ܡܛܠܬܗ܇ ܥܒܩ ܕܢܝܦܠ. ܐܠܐ ܟܕ ܠܟܠܗܝܢ ܢܝܛܪ. ܐܩܝܡ ܐܢܝܢ. ܘܫܠܡ ܐܢܝܢ. ܡܫܝܚܐ
Christ, who said of himself: 'It is fitting for us to fulfill all righteousness,' did not let anything fall that concerns the exactitude regarding him; but, after having observed all things, he consolidated them and fulfilled them, for Christ is the beginning and the end of the Law (νόμος)


[p. 313] {#po.8.p313}
ܓܝܪ ܫܘܪܝܐ ܗܘ ܘܫܘܠܡܐ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ ܘܕܢܒ̈ܝܐ. ܘܐܠܗܐ ܕܕܝܐܬܝܩܐ ܥܬܝܩܬܐ ܘܚܕܬܐ. ܗܽܘ. ܕܐܦ ܒܝܕ ܒܝܣܪܐ ܕܢܝܚ ܠܢ ܒܝܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܝܐ. ܠܗ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܘܐܘܚܕܢܐ ܥܡ ܐܒܐ ܘܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ. ܐܡܝܢ܀
* and of the Prophets and the God of the old and the new Alliance (διαθήκη), and he rose for us also in the flesh in these last days. To him be praise and power with the Father and the Holy Spirit in all ages. Amen.

### END OF HOMILY LXIII. * HOMILY LXIV
ܫܠܡ ܡܐܡܪܐ ܕܫܬܝܢ ܘܬܠܬܐ܀ ܡܐܡܪܐ ܕܫܬܝܢ ܘܐܪܒܥܐ

### AS THE FAITHFUL HAD ASKED THAT THE HOMILY PRONOUNCED AT CYR AGAINST THE BLASPHEMIES OF THEODORET BE REPEATED TO THEM, THIS LATTER HOMILY
ܟܕ ܐܠܘ ܐܚ̈ܐ ܡܗܝ̈ܡܢܐ. ܕܡܐܡܪܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܐܡܪ ܒܩܘܪܘܣ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܓܘܕܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܬܐܘܕܘܪܝܛܘܣ ܢܬܬܘܕܥ ܠܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܫܘܪܝܗ ܐܬܠܝܡ ܠܕܘܟܪܢܐ ܗܘ ܕܡܫ̈ܬܡܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܛܝ̈ܠܝܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܬܩܛܠܘ ܡܢ ܗܪܘܕܝܣ. ܐܬܬܘܣܦ ܗܼܘ ܗܢܐ ܡܐܡܪܐ. ܘܐܦ ܟܕ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܡܕܡ ܡܕܡ ܐܬܬܘܣܦ. ܐܬܐܡܪ ܕܝܢ ܒܥܕܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܬܩܪܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܩܐܛܐܩܐܝܢܝܢ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܟܚܕܬܐ. ܐܝܟܐ ܕܚܠܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܬܥܠ̈ܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܗܪܣܝܣ ܗܿܝ ܢܣܛܘܪܐܝܢܝܬܐ܀
WAS ADDED, AFTER THE BEGINNING HAD BEEN ADAPTED TO THE COMMEMORATION THAT WAS BEING CELEBRATED OF THE CHILDREN PUT TO DEATH BY HEROD, AND AFTER SOME ADDITIONS HAD BEEN MADE TO IT. IT WAS PRONOUNCED IN THE CHURCH CALLED κατὰ καινήν, THAT IS TO SAY "WHICH
ܣܕ ܨܒܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܫܦܝܪ ܕܬܕܥܘ. ܕܐܦ ܝܘܡܝܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ ܡܢ ܟܕܘ. ܩܠܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܐܕܪܥ ܥܠ ܛܝ̈ܠܝܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܫܒܪ̈ܐ. ܕܚܠܦ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܢ ܗܪܘܕܝܣ
IS IN THE NEW (CITY)", WHERE THESE FOXES OF THE NESTORIAN HERESY (αἴρεσις) WERE SLIPPING IN. I would like, know it well, today again as in the past already, to raise my voice over the very little children put to death by Herod for the


[p. 314] {#po.8.p314}
ܐܬܩܛܠܘ. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܝܕ ܢܟܣܬܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܡܫܒܚܐ. ܠܓܒܪܐ ܡܫܡܠܝܐ ܡܢ ܟܠܝܗ̇ ܫܝܘܪܘ ܡܛܘ. ܠܡܫܟܚܬܐ ܕܩܘܡܬܐ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܟܕ ܣܗܕܘ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܚܝܠܬܢܐ
Christ. By such a praiseworthy immolation they arrived in an unforeseen impulse to the perfect man, to the measure of spiritual age and they attested that he was the powerful God, the child of Bethlehem who is born for us and not indeed for himself, as the prophet also cries
ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܐܫܥܝܐ ܢܒܝܐ ܡܙܥܩ. ܕܛܠܝܐ ܐܬܝܠܕ ܠܢ. ܗܼܘ ܓܝܪ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܐܦ ܒܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܒܐ. ܡܠܬܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܦ ܐܬܝܗܒ ܠܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ
Isaiah: 'A child is born to us.' The same indeed was still the Son of the Father, the unique Word who was given to us, because he delivered himself to God the Father for us as a sacrifice and an offering of sweet odor.
ܚܠܦܝܢ ܐܫܠܡ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܠܪ̈ܝܚܐ ܒܣܝܡܐ. ܕܒܚܬܐ ܘܩܘܪܒܢܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܘܐܒܐ܀ ܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܩܕܡ ܕܒܝܚܬܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܕܐ ܩܕܡܘ ܐܬܩܛܠܘ. ܡܢܘ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܠ ܛܘܒܐ. ܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܡܝܠܐ ܐܓܪܐ ܐܫܟܚܘ ܠܗܘܢ. ܘܕܐܓܪܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ. ܗܿܝ ܟܝܬ ܕܐܦ ܐܪܡܝܐ ܗܿܘ ܢܒܝܐ. ܓܕܠܝܐܝܬ ܩܕܡ ܐܡܝܪܗܿ. ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܡܪ ܕܪܚܝܠ ܟܕ ܠܒܢ̈ܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ̇ ܐܠܝܐ ܗܘܬ: ܠܐ ܨܒܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܡܬܒܝܐܘ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ: ܟܕ ܪܚܝܠ ܠܒܝܬ ܠܚܡ ܩܪܐ ܗܘܐ: ܡܛܠ ܕܬܡܢ ܩܒܝܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܪܚܝܠ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܝܥܩܘܒ: ܗܽܘ ܕܐܦ ܐܡܝܪ ܒܝܕܘܟܡ ܡܛܠܬܗܿ: ܘܩܒܪܬܗܿ ܬܡܢ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܐܝܦܘܕܪܘܡܘܢ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢܟ ܕܒܝܬ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܪ̈ܟܫܐ: ܗܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܒܝܬ ܠܚܡ
These little children who were put to death before this sacrifice, * who then would not call them blessed? For they found for themselves a death full of reward, and (full) of the great reward of the kingdom of heaven; this is also what the prophet Jeremiah clearly predicted. Indeed, he (first) said: 'Rachel who was weeping for her children refused to be comforted, because they are no more,' by giving the name of Rachel to Bethlehem, because it was there that Rachel, wife of Jacob, was buried; - the latter said somewhere about her: * 'I buried her there on the road to the hippodrome, that is to say of the hippodrome, which is Bethlehem' - he added then: 'Thus speaks the Eternal: Retain the


[p. 315] {#po.8.p315}
ܐܚܬܝ ܒܬܪܟܝ ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܥܝ̈ܢܝܟܝ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘ ܐܓܪܐ ܠܒܢ̈ܝܟܝ ܐܡܿܪ ܡܪܝܐ. ܛܒܐܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܡܦܫܩܢܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܠܘ ܠܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܕܝܠܟܝ: ܐܠܐ ܠܒ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܝܠܟܝ ܦܝܫܩ. ܫܒܘܩ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܦ ܠܥܒܕܐ ܕܐܡܐ ܢܚܫܒܝܗ̇ ܐܢܫ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܠܗܿܝ ܕܠܟ̈ܢܫܐ ܒܝܫ̈ܝܬܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ̇ ܬܘܟܬܫ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܢܝܕܟܣܬܐ ܕܚܠܦ ܫܦܝܪܘܬ ܕܚܝܼܠܬܐ. ܐܝܢܐ ܘܟܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܓܪܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝܐ ܕܝܠܗܿ. ܬܘܒ ܗܼܘ ܢܒܝܐ ܐܸܡܿܪ. ܢܝܼܦܢܘܢ ܡܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒܝ̈ܒܐ. ܘܢܗܘܐ ܣܒܪܐ ܠܚܪܬܟܝ ܐܸܡܿܪ ܡܪܝܐ. ܘܢܗܦܟܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝܟܝ ܠܬܚܘܡ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܥܕܒܝܩܘ ܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܐܪܥܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒܝ̈ܒܝܗܘܢ: ܕܦ̈ܠܚܐ ܕܗܪܘܕܣ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܩܶܛܠܝ ܛ̈ܠܝܐ. ܗܦܟܘ ܠܬܚܘܡܝܗܘܢ. ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܐܦ ܕܟܠܢ. ܦܪܕܝܣܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܗܽܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܘܒܝܬ ܐܒܗ̈ܐ ܕܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܟܝܬ ܕܡܕܝܢ ܟܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܢ ܒܬܩܐܒܘܬܐ ܗܕܐ. ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܥܡܪܝܢ ܚܢܢ. ܐܝܟ ܛܪ̈ܝܕܐ ܘܕܫܝܕܝܢ ܠܐܟܣܘܪܝܐ. ܘܗܢܐ ܣܒܪܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܚܪܬܢ. ܦܘܢܝܐ ܛܘܒܬܢܐ. ܕܠܘܬ
lamentations of your voice and the tears of your eyes; for there will be a reward for your children, says the Eternal.' Another translator indeed translated not "your works", but "your children". Concede however that one could think that it is also the "work" of the mother to have delivered her own children to immolation for piety. What then is the reward of her children? The prophet says also himself: 'They will return from the land of the enemies and there will be hope for your end, says the Eternal, and your children will return to their territory.' Indeed, after having left the land of their enemies, soldiers of Herod who put the children to death, the very little children returned to their territory, and their territory like ours to all is Paradise, the land and the paternal house of the first father, in such a way therefore that, as long as we are * in this sojourn, we remain outside our territory as subjects chased and expelled into exile (ἐξορία). We have after our end this hope, which is the blessed return toward this territory, which these children obtained quickly.
ܗܠܝܢ. ܗܽܘ ܕܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܐܝܬܘܝܘ ܠܗ ܛ̈ܠܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ. ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܗܘܟܝܠ ܢܚܘܣ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܐܚܟ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܬܚܢܢܝܬܐ ܚܝܼܦܘ. ܡܐܝܠܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܝ̈ܬܢܐ ܢܚܫܘܒ ܐܢܘܢ. ܕܗܼܘܘ ܩܕܡ̈ܝܝ ܩܘܡܐ ܕܓܘܕܐ ܕܣܗ̈ܕܐ
One will not therefore take pity on them as those who have endured sufferings worthy of pity, but rather one will deem them blessed,


[p. 316] {#po.8.p316}
ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܚܝܼܦܘ ܚܠܦ ܡܫܝܚܐ. ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܦ ܟܠܗ ܡܐܡܪܐ ܨܒܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܼܡܪܬ ܕܐܩܪܒ ܠܗܘܢ. ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥ
because they became the first-born of the choir of martyrs who suffered for Christ.
ܐܢܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܩܐ ܫܒܝܠܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܢܓܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܝ. ܘܪ̈ܓܓܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܬܫܡܥܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܩܘܪܘܣ ܐܬܐܡܪ. ܗܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܫܡܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܗܿܝ. ܠܘܩܒܠ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܡܦܠܓܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܗܘ ܚܕ ܡܪܢ ܘܐܠܗܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ. ܒܥܦܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܗܳܝ ܕܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܚܕܝܘܬܐ. ܗܝ ܟܫܬ ܕܡܣܬܒܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܝ ܣܓܝ ܡܥܫܩܢܝܬܐ ܘܒܛܝܠܬܐ. ܐܠܘܠܐ ܕܠܝܘܩܢܐ ܕܡܘܫܐ ܚܕܬܬ ܒܥܝܘܢܝ ܘܠܘܬ ܗ̇ܘ ܚܝܿܪܬ. ܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܗܽܘ ܠܢܡܘܣܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܝܗܒ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܒܚܘܪܝܒ. ܩܪܐ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܬܘܒ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܐܪܒܥܝܢ ܫܢܝܢ. ܗܽܘ ܕܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܛܠܩܘܗܝ. ܟܕ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܢܦܩܘ ܡܢ ܡܨܪܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܐܡܪ ܡܝܬܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܟܕܘܢ. ܠܗ ܟܕ ܠܗ ܠܢܡܘܣܐ ܒܦܣܩ̈ܬܐ ܬܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܒܢ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܟܕ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܕܘܡ ܬܡܢ ܠܐܦ̈ܝ ܡܥܪܒܝܗ̇ ܕܡܘܐܒ ܒܐܪܥܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܩܪܝܒܐ ܠܪ̈ܕܝܘܗܝ ܕܝܘܪܕܢܢ. ܥܡ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܟܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܩܛܝܢܬܐ ܘܡܠܦܢܝܬܐ ܘܓܪܝܕܘܬܐ ܚܬܝܬܬܐ. ܕܡܢ ܗܪܟܐ. ܐܦ ܬܢܝܢ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܟܬܒܐ. ܡܕܝܢ. ܠܘ ܠܪܘܚܩܐ ܡܢ ܝܘܩܢܐ ܗܢܐ ܢܦܠܐ. ܗܿܝ ܕܐܦ ܚܢܢ ܟܕ ܫܒܩܢܢ ܠܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܗܿܝ. ܐܝܕܐ ܕܒܗܿ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܘ ܐ݈ܢܫ̈ܝܢ
I would like, as I have already said, to devote this whole homily to them and I do not know how you draw me into another path and how you desire to hear what was said at Cyr, - it is indeed the name of this city, - against those who divide the only Jesus-Christ our Lord and our God by means of the duality of natures after the union. That would seem to me in truth very debatable and quite useless, if I had not remembered again the image (εἰκών) of Moses and if I had not considered it. This one indeed had read before the children of Israel the Law (νόμος) that God had given them on Horeb, and again after the space of forty years that they had spent in the desert, while all those who had left Egypt so to speak * were already dead, he repeated to the children * of these the same Law (νόμος) briefly, while he was to the west of Moab in the region neighboring the waves of the Jordan, with a short and instructive account and a perfect clarity: from there comes that he also gave to this book the name of "repetition of the Law (Deuteronomium)". Consequently, it does not depart from this


[p. 317] {#po.8.p317}
ܕܛܥܝܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܕܚܠܬ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܗܿܝ ܥܬܝܩܬܐ. ܕܡܨܪ̈ܝܐ ܒܕܘܓܡ̈ܛܐ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܒܫܦܝܪܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܬܐ ܚܬܝܬܬܐ ܗܺܝ ܕܗܟܐ. ܠܗ ܟܕ ܠܗ ܠܡܐܡܪܐ ܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ ܐܗܦܘܟ ܒܣܒ. ܘܗܕܐ ܟܕ ܒܕܘܓܡ̈ܛܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܐ ܕܝܠܟܘܢ ܐܚܢܢ ܕܒܫ̈ܦܥܘܗܝ ܕܝܘܪܕܢܢ ܡܬܦܪܦܥܝܢ ܚܢܢ. ܘܠܘ ܟܕ ܩܪܝܒܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܟܒܪ ܓܝܪ ܐܦ ܗܪܟܐ ܐܝܬ ܐܢܫ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܨܪܝܐ ܐܚܝܢ ܗܽܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܒܝܪܘܫܠܡ. ܡܗܝܡܢܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܫܢܝܘ ܡܢ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܒܗ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܐܦ ܗܠܝܢ ܢܬܬܘܘܢ ܘܢܒܩܘܢ ܠܬܪܥܝܬܗܘܢ ܒܫܦܝܘܬܐ. ܠܬܪܥܝܬܗܘܢ ܒܫܕܦܬܐ܀
image (εἰκών), that we too, after having left this city in which there were certain men filled with zeal from the point of view of dogmas (δόγματα) for the ancient idolatry of the Egyptians and not for the true present piety, we repeat again the same homily, and that when we are charmed by your orthodox dogmas (δόγματα) as by the waves of the Jordan and not only by our presence (in your midst). Perhaps indeed there is also here someone who is Egyptian like the first by impiety. But we have the confidence that just as these returned from the error of their fathers, so these latter will repent and abandon their evil spirit.
ܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܕܝܢ ܒܡܐܡܪܐ ܗܿܘ: ܗܽܘ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܬܐܡܪ ܡܢܢ ܒܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܩܕܡ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܬܕܟܪܬ: ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܗܽܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ: ܟܕ ܡܘܕܝܢܢ ܒܩܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܒܫܘܙ̈ܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ: ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܩܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܒܪܝܫ ܥܕܬܐ ܕܬܡܢ: ܕܠܐ ܡܒܨܪܐܝܬ ܘܠܐ ܩܦܝܬܐܝܬ° ܠܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܣܡ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ.. ܗܟܢܐ܀
Here is the homily that we pronounced in the city mentioned a little higher up, - after the first sentence where we thanked from the beginning the one who was at the head of this Church for having served his faithful the word of God without diminution and without alteration:
Because we have seen, etc. ...
ܠܘܬ ܐܓܘ̈ܢܐ ܪܘܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܙܕܩܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ°܀ ܗܟܢܐ ܟܝܬ ܐܦ ܒܚܕܚܕ̈ܢܐ ܡܢ ܨܚܝ̈ܚܐ ܕܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ ܟܬܝܒ: ܕܫ̈ܠܬܐ ܡܕܡ
In some manuscripts of the Gospel (Εὐαγγέλιον) it is also written:


[p. 318] {#po.8.p318}
ܕܕܡܐ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܬܡܝܗܐܝܬ: ܒܕܡܘܬ ܛܘ̈ܦܐ ܕܕܘܥܬܐ ܙܕܝܢ ܗܘܝ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܢܘܒܫ.. ܘܐܝܟ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܿܦܩ ܘܛܿܪܕ ܠܗܿ ܠܡܥܝܢܐ ܕܕܚܠܬܐ. ܘܐܢܕܝܢ ܠܐ܂. ܐܠܘ ܠܘ ܗܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܕܐܝܟ ܕܒܪܐܙܐ ܡܣܬܥܪܐ܂. ܘܐܦܝܠܐ ܐܠܘ ܐܢܫ ܫܟܝܦܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܘܕܚܝܠ ܡܢ ܟܠܢܫ ܕܚܘܠܬܢ:. ܡܢ ܡܕܟܦܠܢܘܬܐ ܘܡܚܝܠܘܬܐ ܒܠܚܘܕܝܗܿ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܟܝܢܐ:. ܒܢܘ̈ܛܦܬܐ ܕܕܡܐ:. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܒܬܠܝܠܘܬܐ
Drops of blood flowed from his body in a surprising manner like drops of sweat so that he might be dried of our nature and that he might become similar to the one who rejects and chases away the source of fear. Otherwise, if this had not happened in a symbolic manner and if he were not more timid and more fearful than everyone because of the humiliation * and the weakness that are outside of nature, he should have been wetted by the drops of blood as by the humidity of sweat.
ܕܕܘܥܬܐ ܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܬܪܛܒ܀ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿܢ. ܘܐܦ ܗܝ ܗܝ ܕܐܡܝܪܐ܇ ܗܿܝ ܕܐܦ ܗܝ ܠܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܐܬܗܝܡܢܬ:. ܕܕܓܠܐܝܬ ܒܚܕܚܕ̈ܢܐ ܡܢ ܐܘܢܓܠܝܐ ܡܿܘܣܦܐ ܟܬܝܒܐ܆. ܕܡܠܐܟܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܕܩܐܡ ܠܘܬ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܡܚܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ. ܘܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܛܠ ܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܛܠܬܢ ܡܣܬܥܪܐ ܗܘܬ. ܒܐܓܘ̈ܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܕܚܠܦ ܫܦܝܪܘܬ ܕܚܠܬܐ: ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܒܪܝ ܠܢ.. ܟܕ ܐܣܗܕ ܩܕܡ ܦܘܢܛܝܘܣ ܦܝܠܐܛܘܣ ܡܘܕܝܢܘܬܐ ܫܦܝܪܬܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ. ܕܐܡܪ ܫܠܝܚܐ.. ܘܟܕ ܡܘܕܥ ܡܢ ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܒܘ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܕܢܬܟܬܟܘܢ ܬܟܝ̈ܬܘܫܐ ܟܗ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ̈. ܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܡܥܕܪܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ. ܐܢ ܓܝܪ ܗܼܘ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ: ܚܟܡܬܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܣܦܩܬ ܠܟܠ
Such is also what is said, - what many believe to have been added wrongly in some * Evangeliaries (Εὐαγγέλιον): - 'There was an angel who stood by the cross and who strengthened him.' For this also took place as a result of a provision made because of us. Indeed, in the combats (ἀγών) for piety as also in the others, he himself delivered us when he made a good confession before Pontius Pilate as the Apostle says, and when he showed by his own example that those who must sustain sacred and holy combats will have the angels to assist them. For if he himself were the wisdom capable of everything and the strength of the Father, how did he have need of a


[p. 319] {#po.8.p319}
ܘܚܝܠܐ ܕܐܒܐ܂. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܣܢܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܥܠ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܚܕ ܕܡܚܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ.. ܗܽܘ ܕܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܕܡܫܡܫܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܡܪܐ ܘܥܒܘܕܐ. ܐܦ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܥܡ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܐܢܫܐܝܬ ܡܬܗܿܦܟ ܗܘܐ. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܪ ܡܬܝ ܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ ܩܪܒܘ܂ ܘܡܫܡܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ. ܘܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܡܕܒܪܢܐܝܬ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܘܡܬܢܚܬܢܐܝܬ ܡܡܿܠܠ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ܂. ܐܘ ܣܒܪ ܐܢܬ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܐ̱ܢܐ: ܗܟܐ ܕܐܦܝܣ ܠܐܒܝ.. ܘܢܫܕܪ ܠܩܬܝ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ̈ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܬܪܬܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܠܓܝܘ̈ܢܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܢܬܡܿܠܘܢ ܟܬܒܝ̈ܐ:. ܕܗܟܢܐ ܙܕܩ ܕܢܗܘܐ.° ܘܐܦ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܢܝܣܛܘܪܝܘܣ ܪ̈ܘܝܢ.. ܗܢܝܙܐܝܬ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܕܐܦܢܐܠ. ܕܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܠܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ: ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܗܘܢ ܕܥܡܪ ܒܗ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܥܠ ܥܘܕܪܢܐ ܕܚܕ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܣܢܝܩ ܗܘܐ. ܐܪܐ ܒܗܘ ܙܒܢܐ: ܣܦܝܩܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܥܡܘܪܝܐ.. ܐܘ ܗܘ ܕܥܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ ܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܘܬ ܣܘܝܥܐ. ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܝܗܝܢ ܓܝܪ.. ܟܠܗ̇ ܐܢܢܩܐ ܕܬܐܡܪܘܢ. ܐܬܬܥܝܪܘ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܘܕܥܘ ܡܟܝܠ.. ܕܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܒܟܘܢ ܢܦܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܒܪܝܫܐ ܓܕܠܝܐ ܡܓܕܦܢܐܝܬ ܘܝܗܘܕܐܝܬ ܦܛܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ܇. ܠܗܽܘ ܕܐܬܨܠܒ ܚܠܦܝܢ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܗܘ ܡܣܝܒܐ
an angel to strengthen him, he who had all the angels at his service as Lord and Creator, even when he lived humanly in the midst of men? And behold, Matthew says in effect, the angels approached and served him, speaking all at once in accordance with the economy and following humility, he also said: Do you think that I cannot now pray to my Father who would send me numerous (helpers), more than twelve legions of angels? How then would the Scriptures be fulfilled, according to which it must be so? It was agreeable to me to pose this question to those who are intoxicated by the ideas of Nestorius: If Christ possessed the Word of God who dwelt in him, according to their expression, how did he have need of the help of an angel? At that moment, was he deprived of the indwelling? Or was the one who dwelt in him not capable of helping him? For it is of absolute necessity that you say one or the other. Wake up then, O you, and know henceforth that you yourselves fall upon yourselves, that you are irrational on a capital and certain point by saying that the one who was crucified for us is a pure and simple man, and that you blaspheme and speak like the Jews. For the impure Theodoret does not blush to say in his refutation


[p. 320] {#po.8.p320}
ܬܐܘܕܘܪܝܛܘܣ. ܠܐ ܡܬܚܡܨ. ܕܒܣܩܘ̈ܒܠܝܘܬ ܡܠܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܘܩܒܠ ܩܝ̈ܦܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܚܟܝܡܐ ܩܘܪܝܠܘܣ܆. ܣܢܝܩ ܥܠ ܥܘܕܪܢܐ ܡܠܐܟܝܐ ܢܐܡܪܝܘܗܝ܆. ܘܒܕܡܒܩܘܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܓܚܟ ܘܣܒܟ ܥܠ ܚܒܪܗ ܢܫܿܐܠ.. ܡܝܼܢܘ ܗܽܘ ܕܿܥܠ ܣܘܝܥܹܐ. ܡܠܐܟܝܐ ܐܣܝܼܬܢܩ. ܗܕܐ ܐܦ ܥܠ ܐܸܣ̈ܐ ܨܪܘܗܿ ܒܝܕ ܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܨܝܪ̈ܐ܇ ܟܕ ܝܘܩܝ̈ܢܐ ܥܒܕܘ ܡܕܡܝܢܐܝܬ܇. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܗܼܢܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܼܢܝܢ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܦܝܩܪܘ. ܘܣܒܪܝܢ ܕܡܒܙܚܝܢ ܠܡܚܝܠܘܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ. ܠܗ̇ܝ ܕܚܢܢ ܡܕܲܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܚܟܝܡܬܐ ܘܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܚܘܝܢܗ̇ܿ. ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܬܐ ܕܢܲܣܒ ܡܚܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܘܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܕܝܠܢ: ܗܿܝ ܕܐܸܡܿܪ ܟܬܒܐ ܟܗܢܝܐ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܐܨܦ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܬܒܪ ܡܚܝܠܐ܆. ܘܚܣܕܐܼ ܢܕܘܢ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܡܘܟܟܐܼ
of the "Chapters (κεφάλαια)" of the wise Cyril that Christ has need of the help of an angel and to pose the following question while attacking his competitor with mockery: "Who is it that had need of the help of an angel? They even represented this on the walls by the art of painters, when they made images (εἰκών) by way of imitation, those whom the same things have rendered furious like the latter, and who think to deride the weakness of Christ which we have shown to be a wise and divine disposition. He who came in effect to take our infirmities and our diseases as the divine Book says, how was he to care that he passed for weak and that we judge that the humiliation * which he took upon himself because of us is a dishonor?
ܗܽܘ ° ܕܡܛܠܬܢ ܩܝܲܒܠ ܥܠܘܗܝ܀ ܒܕܟܘܢ ܐܦ ܥܠ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܒܗ
That is why it is also for us that he groaned, etc. …

### END OF HOMILY LXIV. HOMILY LXV
ܫܠܡ ܡܐܡܪܐ ܫܬܝܢ ܘܐܪܒܥܐ܀


[p. 321] {#po.8.p321}
ܥܠ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܒܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܘܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ. ܡܘ̈ܣܦܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܝܪ̈ܢ ܡܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܩܠܝܠ ܠܰܦܝ ܫܘܠܡܗ܆ ܐܦ ܥܠ ܠܒܝܫ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܓܢܐܛܝܘܣ܀
ON SAINT BASIL AND SAINT GREGORY. WE REPORT AGAIN, TOWARD THE END (OF THE HOMILY), SOME SHORT WORDS ON IGNATIUS CLOTHED WITH GOD.
ܣܗ ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܟܕ ܒܢܡܘܣܐ ܕܩܘ̈ܠܣܐ ܚܐܪ ܒܗܘܢܗ. ܢܚܣܟܐ ܟܕ ܢܚܙܐ ܕܒܗܕܐ ܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܥܒܪܬ ܠܡܨܥܬܐ:. ܐܝܟ ܐܝܕܐ ܕܐܝܬ ° ܠܗܘܢ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܐܡ̈ܪܐ ܚܝ̈ܓܢܝܐ ° ܐܡܿܪܝܢ:. ܘܒܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܠܩܘ̈ܠܣܐ ܙܩܪܝܢ. ܗܢܘܢ ܕܚܕ ܠܒܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܒܨܝܦܘܬܐ. ܗ̇ܝ ܕܒܝܪܒܝܽܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܘܒܫܘܦܪܗܘܢ ܕܫܡ̈ܗܐ܇. ܢܥܠܘܢ ܘܢܘܪܒܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ. ܘܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܥܠ ܦܓܖ̈ܐ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ:. ܟܘܬܝܢܐ ܡܥܠܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܫܦܝܪܘܬ ܠܫܢܐ ܢܥܛܦܝܘܢ܇. ܘܫܦܝܪ̈ܐ ܢܚܘܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܒܪ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܘ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܟܝܢܐ:. ܘܐܘ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ ܐܘ ܒܨܝܪ ܣܓܝ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܛܝܫܐ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܚܣܝܪܝܢ. ܒܪܘܚܩܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇
Let no one, considering in his intelligence the rule (νόμος) of panegyrics, pass judgment on me that it is with this thought that I have come forward in public, as do * those who pronounce solemn speeches * and who compose panegyrics according to the rules of art; they have only one concern and one preoccupation, to exalt the facts by the greatness of thoughts and by the beauty of expressions, to give them (thus) majesty, to clothe them like naked bodies with the sublime garment of a beautiful language and to show them beautiful by the exterior aspect, while by nature they are not so or they lack totally or at least considerably the aspect that they falsely attribute to them. This thought is indeed very far from me; for, even in the case where I would have had the wrong of


[p. 322] {#po.8.p322}
ܗܕܐ. ܐܡܬܝ ܕܐܦܢ ܗܟܢܐ ܒܫܕܦܐܝܬ ܣܕܝܡ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܢܐܼ.. ܒܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܘܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ: ܟܕ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܬܪ̈ܥܝܬܐ ܕܟܠܢܫ ܟܬܝ̈ܒܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܝܘܬܐ: ܘܕܘܒܪܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܐ ܘܕܡܫܠܚ ܡܢ ܟܠܗ ܡܝܣܟ ܒܐܦܐ
putting myself to this point of view, Basil and Gregory, since they have in all minds written and immortal monuments of virtues and have led an impassible life exempt from all hypocrisy, following the example of the blessed inhabitant of paradise, would not have need of my garments of skin; for there is indeed need of them only for those who have lived a life exterior and foreign to paradise.
ܕܝܠܝ.. ܠܐ ܣܢܝܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܣܢܝܩܝܢ.. ܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܕܚܝܐ ܕܠܒܪ ܘܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܦܪܕܝܣܐ ܚܝܘ܀ ܗܿܝ ܕܝܢ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܬܝܨܦܐ ܠܝ.. ܗܿܝ ܕܒܟܠ ܫܢܬܐ ܟܕ ܡܛܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܡܝܩܪܐ܇. ܗܽܘ ܕܒܗ ܪܒܐ ܒܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܐܫܬܩܠ ܠܘܬ ܡܡܛܠܠܐ ܟܡܝܢܝܬܐ. ܐܣܝܡ ܒܡܨܥܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܟܠܢܫ ܘܐܙܝܚ ܠܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ. ܒܕܡܝܼܘܬ ܟܘܠܝܘܬ ܙܝܢܐ ܫܒܝܚܬܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܛܪ̈ܩܐ ܕܐܬܢܨܝܚܘ. ܐܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܠܘܚܐ ܢܡܘܣܝܬܐ:. ܐܘ ܐܝܟ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ܇. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܓܢܒܪܬܐ ܘܢܡܘܣܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܗܘܠܢܝܬܐ ܢܬܥܠܐ. ܐܘ ܓܝܪ ܠܘ ܟܘܠܝܘܬ ܙܝܢܐ ܗܘ ܕܘܒܪܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܝܟ ܦܘܠܘܣ܆. ܐܟܚܕܘ ܗܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܙܝܢܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܐܘ ܠܘ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ܇. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܠܥܒܕܗ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܬܘܒ ܕܫܠܝܚܐ:. ܚܘܝܘܗܝ ܟܬܝܒܐ ܒܠܒܝܗܘܢ. ܐܘ ܠܘ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ ܙܕܩ ܕܢܝܣܒܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܕܒܚܩܠܘ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ
But what preoccupies me particularly is that each year, when arrives the glorious day where the great Basil departed for the heavenly tabernacles, I must appear in public before everyone and celebrate their place, following the example of the glorious panoply of valiant and illustrious men * or of the tables of the Law (νόμος) or of the exemplar (τύπος) of the cross, in order to raise ourselves to the heroic, legal, and immaterial thought. Is it not a panoply, in effect, the life of these men who like Paul have made of their members an armor of justice for God? Or are they not the Law (νόμος), they who have shown the work of the Law (νόμος) written in their hearts, still according to the word of the Apostle? Or must one not consider them as an


[p. 323] {#po.8.p323}
ܠܡܝܼܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܘܢܝܼܩܦܘ ܒܬܪ ܡܫܝܚܐ܆. ܘܦܠܝܚܐܝܬ ܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܐܙܥܝܼܩܘ. ܕܠܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܨܠܝܒ܆. ܠܥܠܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܚܢܢ. ܘܠܐ ܚܝܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܡܿܢ ܬܘܒ܆. ܚܝ ܕܝܢ ܒܢ ܡܫܝܚܐ܆. ܗܽܘ ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܫܐ ܚ̇ܝܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܒܒܣܪ܇ ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܚܝܝܢܢ ܗܝ ܕܒܪܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ܀
exemplar (τύπος) of the cross, they who have carried the cross, that is to say the mortal condition of this world, and who have set themselves to follow Christ, by crying out also like the Apostle: The world is crucified for us, as we are for the world. It is no longer we who live, it is Christ who lives in us; what we live now in the flesh, we live in the faith of the Son * of God
ܡܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܚܪܢܝܐ܆. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܡܓܿܕܠܐ ܪܘܪ̈ܒܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܬܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܘܡܬܟ̈ܬܫܢܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܥܕܬܐ ܒܐܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܘܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ܆. ܚܝܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܢܫܝܡܝܢ܇. ܐܠܐ ܐܸܢ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ. ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܢܐܚܐ܆ ܐܢܫ܆ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܢܐܚܐ܆. ܕܝܬܝܪ ܪܒܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ ܠܝ. ܗܽܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܚܝ܆. ܡܢ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܡܬܠܒܟܡ ܘܡܬܬܪܪ. ܗܽܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܼܝ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܢܫܡ ܘܚܝ܆ ܟܕ ܩܝܫܥܐܝܬ ܩܐܡ ܥܠ ܟܐܦܐ܆. ܡܐܠܠܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܠܐܦܝ̈ܝ ܠܥܠ ܡܬܬܝܬܐ܆. ܠܐ̱ܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܗܼܘ ܢܫܡ ܘܡܡܿܠܠ ܠܒܕ ܕܢܐܚܘܢ܆. ܘܡܗܝ̈ܡܢܐ ܠܒܕ ܠܗܘܢ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܗܼܘ ܦܘܠܘܣ܆ ܗܟܐ ܡܿܢ ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ܆. ܕܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܚܝ. ܗܫܐ ܕܝܢ܆ ܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܗܺܝ ܕܝܬܝܪ
Of what other thing did these great and indestructible towers and these first defenders of the Church, Basil and Gregory, live, and what did they breathe outside of faith? For "to live of faith" seems to me greater than "to live in faith". Indeed, he who lives in faith is still seized and confirmed by faith, but he who breathes faith and lives of it, because he stands on the rock solidly, or rather (μᾶλλον δέ) is carried still toward the higher things, * makes others live of the things that he breathes himself and of which he speaks, and makes them believers. That is why Paul said in certain circumstances that he lived in faith, and in other circumstances where he rose higher, as if he lived of faith, he said that Christ spoke in him
ܪܒܐ ܣܠܩ܆. ܐܝܟ ܡܶܢ ܕܗܼܝ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܚܝ܆. ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܡܿܠܠ ܒܗ ܐܸܡܪ ܗܘܐ܀ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܠܦܢܐ ܚܝ̈ܠܬܢܐ ܕܫܪܪܐ܆. ܕܒܡ̈ܡܠܦܢܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ
Such were these powerful doctors of the truth; by their doctrines as


[p. 324] {#po.8.p324}
ܐܝܟ ܕܒܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܩܛܠܘ ܠܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܒܪܘܫܥܐ ܦܩܝܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܪܬܚܝܢ܇. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܥܒܕܘ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܐܚܘܢ܆ ܚܝ̈ܝܐ ܫܪ̈ܝܪܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܬܪܝܝܨܬܐ. ܟܕ ܐܬܕܡܝܘ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܐܡܿܪ܆. ܐܢܐ ܐܩܛܘܠ ܘܐܚܐ ܐܥܿܒܕ܆ ܐܡܚܐ ܘܐܢܐ ܐܣܐ܆. ܕܐܝܟ ܗܟܢܝܐ ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܗܽܘ ܛܝ̈ܒܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ. ܕܡܩܛܠ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܚܐ܆. ܘܡܿܚܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܐܣܐ. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܠܥܝܢ ܘܡܝܬܝܢ ܡܘܬܐ ܗܘ ܕܠܥܠܡ܆. ܗܼܢܘܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܗܝܢ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܢܡܘܬܘܢ ܐܘ ܕܢܝܼܒܠܥܘܢ. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܙܥܩ܆ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ. ܠܡܢܐ ܡܝܬܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܒܝܬܐ ܕܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ܆. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܨܒܐ ܐ̱ܢܐ ܒܡܘܬܗ ܕܗܽܘ ܕܡܿܐܬ ܐܸܡܿܪ ܐܕܘܢܝ ܡܪܝܐ܇ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܗܦܘܟ ܡܢ ܐܘܪܚܗ ܘܬܚܐ ܢܦܫܗ. ܗܕܐ ܗ̇ܢܘܢ ܡܠ̈ܠܦܢܐ ܕܡܝܠܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ܆. ܐܦ ܗܟܐ ܡܕܥܒܕܝܢ܇. ܒܝܕ ܥܼܡܠܐ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܦܫܩܘ ܒܡܟܿܬܒ̈ܢܘܬܗܘܢ. ܟܠ ܡܿܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܩܪܐ ܒܗܘܢ܆. ܡܿܐܼܬ ܡܶܢ ܠܒܪܢܫܐ ܥܬܝܩܐ܆. ܗܽܘ ܕܚܝܚ ܒܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܕܒܝܼܣܪܐ܆. ܚܝܝܐ ܕܝܢ
well as by arrows they put to death many men whom wickedness rendered furious and ardent and then they made them live of the true life of the orthodox faith and in this manner they imitated God who says: I will put to death and I will make live, I will strike and I will heal. Such is indeed the good God who puts to death to make live and who strikes to heal. For those who perish and die of the eternal death are for themselves the cause of this kind of death or loss and it is to them that God cries these words: Why do you die, house of Israel? For I do not want the death of him who dies, says the Lord God, but (I desire) that he return from his way and that his soul live. This is what these doctors of the words of the Spirit do even now by means of the works they have left in their writings. Whoever reads them indeed dies * to the old man who lives in the spirit of the flesh, and lives to the new man, created according to God in a true justice and holiness and renewed in the knowledge according to the image of him who created him
ܠܗܰܘ ܚܕܬܐ܆. ܠܗܘ ܕܐܬܒܪܝ ܒܐܠܗܐ܆. ܒܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܘܒܚܝܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܫܪܪܐ܆. ܘܡܬܚܕܬܐ ܒܡܫܬܘܕܥܢܘܬܐ ܒܨܠܡܐ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܒܪܝܗܝ܀ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܢܐ܆ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܐܢܐ ܠܝ ܐܛܥܐ܆. ܘܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܒܝܫܘܬܗ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܕܗܫܐ
I myself would delude myself (easily) and, when I consider the wickedness of the present time, I would think I was someone, although in reality I am absolutely nothing. But, by fixing the eye of my spirit on these


[p. 325] {#po.8.p325}
ܚܐܪ ܐܢܐ܆. ܐܣܝܼܒܪ ܕܐܢܫ ܐܝܬܝ܆ ܟܕ ܒܫܪܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܬܝ܆. ܟܕ ܒܗܿܢܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܡܚܙܝܬܐ ܡܿܩܬ ܐܢܐ ܥܝܢܐ ܕܬܪܥܝܬܝ܆. ܡܫܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܫܛܝܘܬܝ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܝ܆. ܘܠܣܕܢܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܝ ܕܕܐܝܟ ܐܝܕܐ ܗܝ܆. ܘܡܬܚܦܐ ܐܢܐ ܘܡܬܚܡܨ ܐܢܐ܆. ܘܠܘܬ ܬܘܪܨܐ ܕܗ̇ܢܝܢ ܕܡܬܚܛܝܢ ܡܢܝ ܡܬܐܥܝܪ ܐܢܐ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܐܚܬ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܠܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ ܬܐܘܠܘܓܘܣ ܕܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܡܐܡܝ̈ܪܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܟܬܒ܆. ܕܠܗܕܝܘܛܐ ܡܿܢ ܘܠܝܗܿܘ ܕܒܥܡܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ܆. ܪܒܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ܆ ܗܿܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܝܫܐ. ܠܪܝܫ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܘܕܒܪܝܫ ܥܡܐ ܩܐܡ܆. ܗܿܝ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܒܝܫܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ. ܗܳܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܡܝܬܪ ܣܓܝ ܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܒܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ܆. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܫܟܚ ܕܠܘܬ ܡܕܡܝܢܘܬܗ ܒܕܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܬܚܝܬ ܐܝܕܗ. ܠܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܝܘܬܪܢܐ ܒܡܠܝܬܐ ܕܡܠܦܢܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܚܐ ܡܿܚܝܐ܆. ܗܿܝ ܕܐܸܡܿܪ ܫܠܝܚܐ܆. ܐܡܬܝ ܕܥܒ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܕܗܘ ܕܡܿܠܦ܆. ܠܘ ܐܟܚܕܐ ܥܡܗ̇ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܡܡܿܠܠܝܢ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܒܫܪܪܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܪ̈ܝܫܝ ܟܗ̈ܢܐ܆. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܙܕܩ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܗܘ̈ܝܢ ܫܟ̈ܝܚܢ ܠܖ̈ܥܘܬܐ ܕܥܪ̈ܒܐ ܡܝ̈ܠܝܠܐ܆. ܐܟܚܕܐ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܢ ܒܗܘܢ. ܙܢܐ. ܡܠܬܐ. ܕܘܒܪܐ. ܪܕܝܘܬܐ ܕܚܕܒܫܬ ܡܪ̈ܕܘܬܐ ܡܫ̈ܚܠܦܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܓܢܣ܆. ܐܦ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܦ ܐܠܗ̈ܝܬܐ܆. ܡܟܠܗܝܢ ܠܘܬ ܬܫܡܫܬ ܕܟܪܘܙܘܬܐ ܐܘܢܓܠܝܬܐ ܐܬܚܝܫܝܬ܀
(doctors) * as on a mirror, I note how great is my folly and what is the ugliness of my soul: I am covered with shame and confusion and I take the resolution to correct my faults. For it must also be remarked that Gregory the Theologian wrote in one of his discourses: "It is important, for the individual (ἰδιώτης) and for him who is part of the people, not to be wicked; but, for the chief of priests and for him who is at the head of the people, it would be on the contrary a great misfortune not to be well above the others by his virtues, in order to be able to draw to his imitation his subordinates. Indeed, there is no profit in the word of teaching which strikes the air as the Apostle says, when the works of him who teaches do not speak with his word. Thus these men were really chiefs of priests, because everything * that must be found in the pastors of spiritual sheep is met in them: the method, the word, the conduct, the instruction which has gathered diverse knowledge of all kinds (γένος) both human and divine and which has used all for (fulfilling) the duty of evangelical preaching.


[p. 326] {#po.8.p326}
ܠܝ ܕܝܢ ܡܣܬܒܪܐ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܫܡ̈ܗܝܗܘܢ ܕܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܕܝܥܬܐ ܕܫܪܪܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܡܫܘ̈ܕܥܢܐ. ܘܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܩܕܡܘ ܐܬܩܪܝܘܢ. ܐܦܢ ܒܢ̈ܝܢܫܐ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܣܝܡܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܫܡ̈ܗܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܡܘܫܐ ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܒܪܗ ܓܝܪܫܡ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ. ܕܬܘܬܒܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܒܐܪܥܐ ܢܘܟܪܝܬܐ. ܐܦ ܗܝ ܕܝܢ ܒܪܬܗ
It seems to me that the names of these men also signify the actions and the reality and that they were given in advance by God, while men impose names after the actions. Thus Moses called his son Gershom, saying: I am an inhabitant in a foreign land, and the daughter of Pharaoh had given to him himself the name of Moses, saying: I have withdrawn him from the waters
ܕܦܪܥܘܢ ܠܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܡܘܫܐ ܫܡܼܗܬ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪܐ. ܕܡܢ ܡ̈ܝܐ ܫܠܝܬܗ܀ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܒܐܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܐܬܦܬܡܗ. ܡܛܠ ܗܝ ܕܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܬܪܥܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܠܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ ܡܿܘܒܠ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܕܡܠܦܢܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܒܡܨܥܬܐ ܕܐܘܪܚܐ ܡܿܥܠܟܝܬܐ ܐܬܝܐ. ܟܕ ܡܟܝ̈ܣܐ ܠܗܪ̈ܣܝܣ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܬ ܫܩ̈ܝܦܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܕܟܐ
It is in the same manner that Basil received his name, because he leads to the kingdom of heaven those of whom he is the pastor, and because the word of his teaching advances in the middle of the royal road by making disappear the heresies (αἱρέσεις) which, placed on either side, find themselves doubly crushed.
ܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܡܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܥܝܪܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܡܥܝܪ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܝܼܢܬܐ ܥܠܡܢܝܬܐ ܕܡܿܟܝܢ. ܘܒܐܝܕܘܗܝ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܫܡܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܗܝ̈ܡܝܢܐ ܥܡ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܡܿܙܥܩ. ܐܢܬܘܢ ܓܝܪ ܟܠܟܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܟܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝܐ ܕܢܝܘܗܪܐ ܘܒܢ̈ܝܐ ܕܐܝܡܡܐ. ܘܠܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܠܠܝܐ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܚܫܘܟܐ. ܐܪܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܢܕܡܟ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܟܪܟܐ. ܐܠܐ ܢܬܬܥܝܪ ܘܢܫܕܗܪ܀
Gregory for his part * seems to awaken those who sleep the sleep of the world to make them watch with God and by means of his name cry to the faithful with Paul: All, indeed, you are the children of light and the children of the day, and we are neither the children of the night nor the children of darkness. Let us not sleep therefore * as do the others, but let us wake up and watch


[p. 327] {#po.8.p327}
ܟܕ ܡܬܒܩܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܚܢܢ ܒܗ ܒܓܒܪܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܒܪܚܝܡܬ ܥܡܝܠܐ. ܕܒܝܗ̇ܝ ܚܬܝܪ ܡܪܢܐܝܬ ܐܬܩܪܝ ܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܥܝܪܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܕ ܥܝܪܐܝܬ ܒܬܐܘܪܝܐ ܐܦ ܠܘܬ ܫܡܝܐ ܪܕܐ ܗܘܐ ܘܒܕܡܘܬ ܚܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܥܠܝܐ ܒܥܝܢܝܐ ܥܝܪܬܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܝܡܐ ܠܘܬ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܚܪ. ܘܡܩܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܘܒܡܬܥܠܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܬܪܥܝܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܓܦܐ ܠܐ ܦܪܚ ܝܬܝܪܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܐܬܚܪܝ ܠܘܬ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪܟܢܝܘܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܚܦܝ ܠܥܝܢܐ ܡܥܩܒܢܝܬܐ ܘܚܪܝܝܬܐ. ܘܡܝܫܚ ܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܒܝܬܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܠܡܬܢܗܪܢܘܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܿܪܝ ܐܘ ܐܬܚܪܝ ܠܘܬ ܥܬܝܪܘܬܗ̇ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܐ. ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܪܥܡ ܠܢ ܬܐܘܠܘܓܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܘܚܬܝܬܬܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܬܥܝܪ ܥܝܪܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܒܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܬܗܘܢܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܗܘ̈ܠܢܝܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܠܗܿܢܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܕܣܘܥܪܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܝܢ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ ܫܡܐ ܕܥܝܪܘܬܐ. ܘܬܝܒ ܓܝܪ ܠܘܬ ܕܢܝܐܝܠ. ܘܗܐ ܥܝܪ ܘܩܕܝܫܐ ܡܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܢܚܬ. ܥܝܪ! ܕܝܢ. ܘܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܡܬܦܫܩ. ܐܠܐ
But by considering in this man especially the love of study, I say that it is in that principally that he was Gregory, that is to say "vigilant". For, while by contemplation (θεωρία) he walked toward heaven in an awakened manner and that in the same manner as the higher powers he looked at the light of the Trinity with an awakened and not sleeping eye, (however) by the penetration and the elevation of his intelligence as well as by wings he did not fly away in an excessive manner and he did not seek to grasp what is inaccessible. But he hid his gaze avid for research and discussions, he regulated the clarity to his own measure and he neither fought nor struggled to obtain the abundance of grace. From there he made us hear a theology (θεολογία) exempt from all error and exact, when he stood in a divine awakening in the likeness of intellectual and immaterial spirits. For they also carry by virtue of their activity the same name of "awakening"; it is written indeed in Daniel: Behold that from heaven descended a vigilant and a holy one. And this word "vigilant" cannot be translated otherwise than by "vigilant" or by "who is in a state of wakefulness".
ܐܢ ܥܝܪܐ ܘܦܝܼܗܪܐ܀ ܒܗ ܟܕ ܒܗ ܥܢܝܢܐ ܐܦ ܠܕܒܝܫ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܓܢܐܛܝܘܣ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܗܫܐ ܗܕܐ ܫܪܘܬܐ
It is in the same manner that Ignatius, clothed with God, who today


[p. 328] {#po.8.p328}
ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܣܡ ܩܕܡܝܢ. ܒܒܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܒܝܚܝܬ ܨܠܝܘܬܐ. ܘܒܡܒܝܬܬܐ ܡܬܩܝ̈ܠܣܢܝܬܐ ܕܬܠܡ̈ܝܕܘܗܝ ܚܕܐ. ܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܚܝܡܐܝܬ. ܡܢ ܗܝ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܥܬܝܕܢ ܩܕܡ ܚܙܐ. ܐܬܦܬܡܗ ܐܝܓܢܐܛܝܘܣ. ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܠܫܝܢܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܝܐ ܐܦܢ ܡܡܫܚܐܝܬ ܡܝܩܦ. ܝܕܥ ܗܘ ܕܢܘܪܢܐ ܕܡܬܓܘܙܠ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܢܐܡܪ. ܡܫܬܘܕܥ ܡܢ ܗܪܟܐ. ܐܝܓܢܡ ܓܝܪ ܡܟܢܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܡܝܐ ܠܢܘܪܐ ܕܩܝܕܝܚܝܐ ܘܡܬܓܘܙܠܐ. ܡܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܒܗ ܓܘܙܠܬܐ ܐܘ ܟܝܬ ܠܐܡܦܝܕܐ ܕܪܚܝܡܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ. ܘܒܪܓܬܐ ܕܗ̇ܝ ܕܚܠܦ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܢܚܫ ܡܫܬܠܗܒ ܗܘܐ. ܗܘ ܕܐܦ ܟܕ ܠܪ̈ܘܡܝܐ ܟܬܒ ܗܘܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ. ܕܢܘܪܐ ܘܚܝ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܙܒܝܘ ܐܕܝܐ ܕ̈ܢܕܐ ܢܐܬܘܢ ܥܠܝ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܠܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܫܬܘܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܟܕ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܡܢ
placed before us this spiritual * feast inside his own church and who rejoices in the praiseworthy virtues of his disciples, also received the name of Ignatius quite appropriately because of the facts themselves, because he knows in advance the future. Whoever has studied the Latin language, even moderately, knows that this name signifies the fire lit, so to speak: for the Latins designate by "ignem" the fire that burns * and which is lit. Who is it that had thus in him the flame, that is to say the torch (λαμπάς) of the love of God and who was inflamed with the desire to suffer for Christ? It is he who said in his letter to the Romans: "Let fire, beasts, tortures of all kinds fall upon me, provided that I obtain Christ," and this when he possessed interiorly the one who loved him, which still astonishes; that is why he also cried out: "He says to me interiorly: Come find my Father"
ܠܓܘ ܠܗܘ ܕܡܬܪܚܡ ܗܘܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܐܦ ܬܡܝܗܐ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܦ ܡܙܥܩ ܗܘܐ. ܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܐܡܿܪ ܠܝ. ܕܬܐ ܠܟ ܠܘܬ ܐܒܝ܀ ܠܘ ܕܝܢ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܒܡܪܢܚܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܐ. ܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܐܬܠܕܚܘܒܬܐ ܠܒܐܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܘܠܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ. ܗܘܬ ܡܕܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܝܓܢܐܛܝܘܣ. ܐܠܐ. ܐܦ
But the resemblance * with Ignatius existed not only in the consideration of the name, which God attributed in advance to Basil and to Gregory, but in their courageous attitude for the truth, in their frankness (παῤῥησία),


[p. 329] {#po.8.p329}
ܒܩܘܡܐ ܙܪܝܙܐ ܕܚܠܦ ܫܪܪܐ. ܒܦܐܪܚܣܝܐ. ܒܐܓܘܢ̈ܐ. ܒܚ̈ܫܐ. ܒܫܘܠܡܘܬܐ ܕܟܪܘܙܘܬܐ. ܐܠܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܡܠܦܝܢ ܗܘܘ: ܠܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܚܠܦܢܐܝܬܐ ܐܬܒܣܪ: ܘܐܨܛܝܠܒ ܚܠܦܝܢ ܘܚܫ ܒܒܣܪ. ܟܕ ܙܥܘܙ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܨܦܘ ܕܣܘܢܗܕܪܘܬܐ ܣܝܡܘܢܝܬܐ ܘܢܘܣܛܘܪܝܢܝܬܐ! ܗܿܝ ܕܡܣܬܟܠܐ܇ ܘܡܝܬܬܩܠܐ ܠܐ ܐܠܗܐܝܬ ܒܚܫܘܬ ܐܠܗܘܬܐ. ܡܦܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܚܫܐ.. ܠܐ ܡܛܐ ܒܙܘܥܐ ܠܐ ܚܫܘܬܐ.. ܐܦܢ ܡܕܒܪܢܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܡܬܒܣܪܐ ܘܡܬܒܪܢܫܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܒܚ̈ܫܐ܆. ܟܕ ܡܕܩܗܐ ܠܥܘܩܣܐ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܢ ܘܕܚܛܝܬܐ. ܘܐܝܓܢܐܛܝܘܣ ܡܢ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ܆ ܐܦܣܘ ܠܝ ܕܡܕܡܝܢܐ ܐܗܘܐ ܕܚܫܐ ܕܐܠܗܝ. ܓܪܝܓܘܪܝܘܣ ܕܝܢ.. ܐܢ ܐܢܫ ܠܐ ܣܓܕ ܠܗܘ ܕܨܠܝܒ.. ܚܪܡܐ ܢܗܘܐ. ܘܢܬܬܣܝܡ ܥܡ ܩܛܠܝ ܐܠܗܐ. ܒܐܣܝܠܝܘܣ ܕܝܢ ܬܘܒ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪܥܐ ܘܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܕܝܡ̈ܡܐ܆ ܘܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܡܝܐ ܡܬܕܝܕ̈ܢ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܝܒܫ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܢܨܒ̈ܬܐ. ܘܟܘܟ̈ܒܐ ܘܐܐܪ ܘܙܒ̈ܢܐ܆ ܘܨܒܝܬܐ ܡܦܬܟܬܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܟܠ܆ ܡܚ̈ܘܝܢ ܠܡܥܠܝܘܬܗ ܕܚܝܠܐ. ܐܚܟܡ ܡܐ ܕܗܝ ܕܢܫܟܚ ܐܠܗܐ ܗܘ
in their combats (ἀγών), in their sufferings, in the accomplishment of their preaching. They knew indeed and they taught that he is God, the Word of God who became incarnate without change, was crucified for us and suffered in the flesh, without worrying much about the blind defense (συνηγορία) of Simon and Nestorius who is scandalized in a manner not at all worthy of God by the suffering of the divinity. They were not ignorant that suffering does not reach the impassible God, although, following the economy, he is in suffering, in that he became incarnate and made man, in order to blunt the sting of death and sin directed against us. Ignatius said: "Let me be the imitator of the sufferings of my God." Gregory said: "If someone does not adore the Crucified, let him be anathema and put in the number of the deicides." Basil said likewise: "All the heaven, all the earth and the myriads of the seas, the creatures that dwell in the waters, as well as the animals and the terrestrial vegetables, the stars, the air, the times and the varied ornament of this universe do not show the greatness * of the strength of God as much as this: that the incomprehensible God could, by the intermediary of the flesh, be united with death in an impassible manner, in such a way that,
ܠܐ ܡܬܥܪܝܢܐ܇ ܕܠܐ ܚܫܘܢܐܝܬ ܒܝܕ ܒܣܪܐ ܢܬܥܪܙܠ ܥܡ ܡܘܬܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܢ ܒܝܕ ܚܫܗ ܢܫܟܢ ܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܠܝܢ ܒܚܫܐ ܗܘ ܕܝܠܝܗ ܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ ܝܗܒܢ.. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܗܘ ܟܕ ܡܡܛܐ܆ ܚܣܪܗ ܠܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ ܘܠܠܐ ܡܬܚܒܠܢܘܬܐ ܘܠܠܐ ܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ.. ܢܦܩܘܢ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܒܚܬܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܐܚܬܝܚܗܘܢ ܐܡܝ̈ܪܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܣܘܢܗܕܪ̈ܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ ܕܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܕܒܝܕ ܗܢܝܢ ܕܠܒܕܝܢ ܒܗܝܢ
by suffering, he procured for us impassibility.” As for the question of knowing how he who, by his own suffering, procured for us impassibility, could himself be deprived of impassibility, * of incorruptibility and of immortality, it is for those who, by their virtues, are the friends of God that it is fitting to resolve it by despising the vain apologies of the impassibility of God the Word and the reasons on which they establish them.


[p. 330] {#po.8.p330}
ܣܘܢܗܕܪܘܬܐ ܡܨܥܪܝܢ܀ ܡܛܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܡ̈ܠܦܢܘܬܐ ܢܦܝܚ̈ܬ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ: ܘܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܟܗ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܫ̈ܠܝܚܝܐ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܠܟܠ ܠܫܢܐ ܠܘܠܐ ܒܥܡܝܢ ܠܕܘܟܪܢܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܣܝܐ ܗܢܘܢ ܡܓܢ ܚܢܢ܆ ܟܕ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܡܙܡܢܝܢܢ ܠܟܘܢ. ܕܟܕ ܓܝܪ ܡܬܩܠܣܝܢ ܙܕܝ̈ܩܐ ܚܕܝܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ. ܐܦ ܟܬܒܐ ܕܡ̈ܬܠܐ ܐܡܪ. ܗܢܘܢ ܕܒܬܟܫ̈ܦܬܗܘܢ ܢܬܠ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܡܠܬܐ: ܗܘ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܗܦܟ ܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ ܒܪܐ ܠܚܝܘܬܐ ܡܠܝܠܬܐ ܒܪܢܫܝܬܐ.. ܕܟܕ ܕܠܐ ܥܩܬܐ ܘܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܗ ܨܒܐ ܢܦܩܝܢܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܚ̈ܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܗܪܟܐ.. ܢܚܕܐ ܘܢܬܒܣܡ ܥܡ ܩܕܝܫ̈ܐ ܐܦ ܒܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ܆ ܠܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܟܝܬ ܘܕܐܒܘܗܝ ܘܕܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܗܫܐ ܘܒܟܠܙܒܢ ܘܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ. ܐܡܝܢ܀ ܫܠܡ ܡܐܡܪܐ ܕܫܬܝܢ ܘܚܡܫܐ܀
It is because of these doctrines inspired by God and these holy and apostolic laws (νόμος) which are a curb for every impious tongue that we celebrate the memory of these saints by preparing for you this spiritual joy. For, says also the book of Proverbs: When one praises the just, the peoples are in joy. May, by their supplications, Christ, God the Word, who created the reasonable being that is man and who created him a second time, grant us, after having passed the life of here below without anguish and according to his good pleasure, to be at the height of joy and felicity also in the kingdom of heaven with the saints, for his own glory and that of the Father and of the Holy Spirit, now, always and in all the centuries. Amen!

### END OF HOMILY LXV
ܡܐܡܪܐ ܕܫܬܝܢ ܘܫܬܐ
* HOMILY LXVI


[p. 331] {#po.8.p331}

### ON THE EPIPHANY
ܥܠ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ܀ ܣܘ ܐܘ ܟܡܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܬܝܗ ܗܘܬ ܛܪܘܢܘܬܐ ܕܥܩܘܒܠܢ ܘܠܣܒܠ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܢ܇ ܘܣܓܝܐܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܗܪܟܐ ܢܒܥܘ. ܡܢ ܕܘܡܪܐ ܓܝܪ ܘܐܡܗܐ ܡܫܝܪܐ ܐܢܐ܆ ܕܥܠ ܘܠܗ ܗܕܐ ܘܕܐܚܟ ܗܕܐ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܣܢܝܩܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܥܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܡܐܣܐ ܘܕܗܘ ܩܢܘܡܗ ܠܒܢ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܥܘܬܪܗ ܕܐܣܝܘܬܐ܆ ܬܬܝܕܥ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܟܘܪܗܢܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ ܠܐ ܝܕܝܥܐ ܗܘܬ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܙܓܕܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܠܐܟܐ܆ ܐܠܐ ܗܘ ܡܪܝܐ ܦܪܩ ܠܢ. ܡܬܕܡܪ ܥܡܢ ܐܫܥܝܐ ܘܡܙܥܩ. ܐܦ ܓܝܪ ܡܠܬܐ ܗܘ ܕܐܠܗܐ: ܟܕ ܝܠܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ: ܚܠܝܕܘܬܐ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪܝ ܘܡܬܘܡܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܐܒܐ: ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܘ ܟܕ ܨܒܐ ܪܚܡܬ ܐܢܫܐܝܬ ܘܡܕܒܪܢܐܝܬ ܕܢܬܝܠܕ ܐܦ ܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ: ܘܢܣܒ ܝܠܝܕܘܬܐ
Oh! what was the tyranny that was exercised against us and against our sin, and what was the multitude of diseases that came out of it. - I am seized with astonishment and stupor, - since there was need of all this healing and of such a healing and of God who heals and acts in person, in such a way that it is after the richness of the healing that was known the greatness of the disease unknown before! For it is neither an ambassador, nor an angel, but it is the Lord himself who saved us, cries out Isaiah, astonished like us. Indeed, while the Word of God was born from on high by nature, I speak of his birth from the Father without beginning and eternal, when he still wanted himself by love for men and in accordance with the economy to be born from below, that he took in the flesh a birth from the Virgin without pain, that by there in a certain manner he began to refashion


[p. 332] {#po.8.p332}
ܡܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܚܝܼܒܠ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܢ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܒܒܣܪ: ܘܒܗܕܐ ܒܙܢܐ ܡܕܡ ܫܪܝ ܕܢܣܘܟܡ ܡܢ ܕܪܝܫ ܠܟܝܢܐ ܕܝܠܢ: ܘܥܡ ܛܝܪܬܐ ܠܢܘܪܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܐ ܒܫܬܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ: ܡܐܠܠܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܠܗܘܬܗ ܬܡܼܡܝܐܝܬ ܠܚܕܐ ܐܚܼܕܘܬܐ: ܘܟܠܐ ܘܐܩܝܡܗ̇ ܠܗܿܝ ܕܒܥܩ̈ܝܬܐ ܬܐܠܕܝܢ ܒܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܥܠ ܚܘܐ ܐܸܬܓܝܼܙܪܬ. ܡܢܐ ܥܒܕ. ܘܠܐܝܟܐ ܢܦܩ. ܠܢ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܟܝܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܝ̈ܠܝܕܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܬܚܬ܇. ܡܕܒܪ ܘܡܦܪܢܣ ܠܗ̇ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܐ
anew our nature by adding in an admirable way to the mud the fire of his own divinity or rather (μᾶλλον δέ) his divinity itself to make only one whole, and that he stopped and lifted the sentence brought against Eve: Thou shalt bring forth in pains, what * does he do and where does he go? He dispenses * and administers freely, to us who are born from below by nature, the birth from on high and from heaven, by crying out to us so to speak by the facts themselves: "I am born from below for that very thing, so that you might be born from on high. That is why I do not make far from pain your mystical birth which took place in a prodigious way in what concerns me. For I, I
